## AutoKernel RVP-C6 executable reward-integrity closure

RVP-C6-6 and RVP-C6-10 are closed with executable gfx90a evidence rather than the earlier static
diff strings and recorded-output fixtures. Research `execution/reward_hack_corpus.py` materializes
**10 planted + 15 clean** standalone HIP programs, compiles all 25 with ROCm 6.2 for gfx90a, and
executes both a normal-128 and anti-short-circuit-127 ranked unit for every program. Each executable
contains a real HIP kernel and an independent host oracle; an expected defect must manifest on both
units, so one-shape, pointer-alignment-no-op, and nondeterministic-timing mutations fail closed.

The immutable r1 receipt is retained as a failed **22/25** attempt. After repairing the three
non-manifesting bodies and adding mutation controls, r2 reached 25/25. Final r3 machine-validates and
binds both earlier attempts by embedded receipt hash plus full-file hash. Its result is **10/10 true
positives, 15/15 true negatives, sensitivity 1.0, specificity 1.0, FPR 0.0, and 25/25 expected runtime
behaviors on both ranked units**. Device claim `akd-006b9b9b049b4249` was held only from
`09:05:03.962937Z` to `09:05:14.248081Z` and released; its sampler recorded 42 samples over 10.283 s
with a 0.250032 s maximum gap. Receipt:
`/mnt/raid0/llm/autokernel/campaigns/rvp-c6-executable-r3-20260812T0905Z/receipt.json`, self-hash
`b4e39c65f23323bd648b84e2b8088850201a2be16b4d46002b8e869a7b1620ee`, file SHA-256
`91bcef0b21220f4947ce1b37bbdfe9df42b645b100f48ec1e71011da2779df9f`.

This is instrument validation for a named exploit taxonomy and hard-case pricing contract. It makes
no arbitrary-program coverage claim and no candidate speed claim. Focused integration passed **508
tests + 122 subtests**; after rebasing on research `accff01f`, the full AutoKernel suite passed
**5,547 tests, 1 expected failure, and 2,241 subtests**. Belief task SC33 records the required
prospective write-side hook; r3 is never retrofitted.

## AutoKernel profiler-tooling correction

The profiler availability audit was committed earlier as `f1cf24e2` but had not reached remote
`main`; integrating it against the newer C6 checkpoint exposed an important conjunction error.
After sourcing `/mnt/raid0/llm/tools/rocm-profilers-6.2/env.sh`, `rocprof` and `rocprofv2` report
ROCm 6.2.0-66 / ROCProfiler 2.0, and `rocm-bandwidth-test` parses its arguments. These tools are
side-loaded deliberately and will appear absent if only the default PATH or `/opt/rocm` is searched.

The first integration incorrectly turned absent `rocprofv3` into a new blocker without reconciling
the later terminal LDS receipt already recorded in this progress log and lower in the same handoff.
Independent audit caught the contradiction immediately. Receipt
`/mnt/raid0/llm/autokernel/probes/inf03-lds-gfx90a-20260811-r4/receipt.json` (SHA-256
`ae1d833c704bdae9a78767d0fc0b927298d6d1dfdb31a0ea11c34058dc525987`) binds successful rocprofv2
commands for both captures: 372 bank and 6,048 phase dispatches, resolving 32 LDS banks and eight
phase cliques of eight lanes. The upstream rocprofv3 spelling was adaptable, not a hard dependency.
The newly introduced checkbox is therefore closed and the stale blocker removed without another GPU
run. This correction is a reminder that availability audits must reconcile terminal receipts before
turning an absent preferred tool into an external dependency.

### Measurements that actually ran

Two, and only two. Both belong to other lanes; they are recorded here because a coordination wrap-up
that reports coordination activity without reporting what compute actually happened is the blind spot
this session spent the night removing.

**MMLU-Pro hardened control (`inference`, GPU).** Three arms on `production-consolidated-v9`, one
exclusive MI210 claim at a time: **A1 93/150**, **A3 88/150**, **A4 plain-Q8 76/150**, zero request
errors. Exact paired McNemar `p=.458` (A1/A3), `.0095` (A1/A4), `.0428` (A3/A4). Manifest stratified
`n=150`, seed `42`, 50 per tier, byte-identical on regeneration. It ships **its own caveat**: A4's
differences are protocol outcomes, not clean latent-quality evidence, because the letter-only control
still produced 34 length-caps at the predeclared 64-token bound. A result that names the reason its
own headline is not the headline is worth more than the headline.

**E5 gemma no-MTP (`mainA`, CPU).** `e5-gemma-nomtp-v9-20260812T0818Z` — 18/18 cells, `error_rate`
0.0, 43/43 requests per cell, observation-grade by construction (13d18h uptime voids decision-grade,
and the warning is recorded rather than defaulted away). The earlier
`e5-gemma-nomtp-v9-20260812T0800Z` is **void, not a second result**: every cell failed at "affinity
preflight exited 1", and it is committed beside the good run carrying a `VOID-DO-NOT-USE.md`, as is
`e5-gemma-crossbinary-repair-20260812T0745Z`. Recording the void runs is the point — banking a zero
silently is the defect they were filed against.

### RETRACTED — "the daemon knew exactly what should run all night and told nobody"

This was the coordinator's headline finding of the session. **It does not survive contact with the
artifacts, and it is withdrawn.**

`mainB` settled it by screening the six dispatched picks with `backlog_row_check.py --ref` before
working any of them. **One of six was dispatchable.** Four (`:463`, `:511`, `:513`, `:628`) had been
`- [x]` since **2026-07-29** — closed fourteen days before the daemon named them — and one (`:626`)
was anchor rot. The file had not been touched in between, so the picker never re-read what closed
them.

Re-derived at wrap-up from the one surviving advisory shard, which is stronger than the original
count and points the same way: **811 `would-assign` records, 100% carrying a `task_id`, resolving to
fifteen distinct `(agent, task, lane)` picks over nine distinct task rows — every one of the nine
from a single file**, `handoffs/active/opendataloader-pipeline-integration.md`. The top six repeat
104–105 times each. The **4,602** figure quoted all night therefore counts *records, not work*: it is
a repetition count of a stuck picker, and it was reported as if it were a backlog.

**Fleet rule adopted from this.** A count of scheduler records is not a count of available work.
State it as: *"N records resolving to M distinct rows, of which K were dispatchable at emission."*
The third number is the only one that was ever a claim about the fleet, and it is the one nobody
computed. This generalises past the bus — any queue-depth, backlog-size or advisory-volume figure
quoted without its distinct-row and dispatchable-at-emission denominators is the same error.

**What survives, and what it is not.** The real defect is a **stuck picker** (C50) — the daemon
serves a stale READY set — and it is *distinct from the authority gag*. Fixing delivery does not fix
selection: a working courier now delivers wrong rows faster. And the `authority: assign` change
still stands, on evidence independent of the retracted finding: the saturation record at epoch 40
carries `ready_depth: 19` with nothing moving, and within a tick of the flip the queue fold reads
**ASSIGNED 6 / CLAIMED 1 / READY 12**, with seven `assigned` and one
`scheduling-recommendation-delivered` advisory record. A retraction is a claim too; it gets less
scrutiny than the claim it withdraws, so this one names exactly what it does and does not take down.

### Corrections made and received

Nine, listed because the pattern is the reusable output: not one of these failed loudly, and not one
was caught by a metric.

1. **Compute duty cycle reported ~20%; actually ~8–9%.** The 19% was scoped to 23:00–01:24Z and then
   quoted as if it covered the night. 00:57–04:44Z carries no compute receipt at all — 3h47m.
2. **Wrong instrument for utilisation.** Instantaneous `rocm-smi` / `ps` sampling was used as a
   utilisation measure; the 15-minute load average (peaking **38.84**) was the right one. The
   sampled bursts were artifacts: `llama-bench` ran 00:00:13–00:11:03, the controls suite
   00:33:47–00:46:23. `ps` %CPU is cumulative, not live.
3. **Checkbox counts used an unanchored `- [ ]` pattern**, matching anywhere in a line and inflating
   both sides. Re-derived: 1273 → 1242 open, 2306 → 2368 done.
4. **A fleet-wide `lane: none` restriction the roster never imposed.** `config.yaml` gives `mainA`
   `[cpu, none]` and `mainB` `[gpu, none]`. The restriction came from task briefs asserting a
   constraint without citing the line it derives from — a second source of truth by construction.
5. **A dispatch list that was 4-of-6 phantom**, drawn from a file whose line 3 reads
   `⛔ SUPERSEDED 2026-08-11 — DO NOT DISPATCH FROM THIS FILE.`
   (`coordination/session-bus/tasks/BACKLOG-DISPATCH-QUEUE.md`).
6. **Three failed merge-verification methods.** Heading-count passed by luck and cannot see content;
   line-set `comm` with stderr suppressed reported **102,881 lost lines** against a real ~1,210,
   because the "not in sorted order" warning went to `/dev/null` and it silently compared garbage;
   added-relative-to-base was the best of the three but line-exact, so rewording read as loss and it
   produced a false two-line alarm.
7. **The 5,292 lane-rejection figure is not reproducible** and is withdrawn. The four affected agents
   are confirmed; the count is not — `advisory.jsonl` was never tracked and its earliest surviving
   record is 08:20:31Z.
8. **A false "live hazard" on `worktrees/`.** `mainD` measured it: `git clean -fdx` removes none of
   them (git skips nested repositories), only `-ffdx` does — and there are **29**, not 20.
9. **A false premise broadcast fleet-wide** — "uncommitted work does not survive a reboot" — used to
   drive flush urgency all night. `/workspace`, the scratch dir and the agent memory dir are all one
   persistent RAID. Committing is still right, as a *concurrency* argument, not a durability one.

### Defects filed — C42 through C50

| ID | Defect | State |
|---|---|---|
| C42 | The supervisor could not tell a daemon running OLD CODE from a healthy one | Fixed (`mainD`), fired in production for the first time 00:45Z |
| C43 | Relaunch races the dying supervisor's lock release — ~90s with nothing watching | Fixed, bounded `flock -w` |
| C44 | Token relay is withdrawal-blind | Fixed `bd2e830d`; verified closed end-to-end at `token-queue.md:365` |
| C45 | Doorbell — nudge guards force conservatism on a channel that never needed payload | Built `777f826e` |
| — | `logs/agent_audit.log` merge tax | Untracked `73998d70` |
| C46 | Hook-vs-skill contradiction: the guard forbade an idiom a project skill instructs | Resolved by the `pkill`-only narrowing (`e08fe836`) |
| C47 | The hook blocked the message *reporting* the incident it guards | Fixed `ee628304` |
| C48 | Supervisor liveness read from a file, not the lock | Fixed `a96191af` |
| C49 | Operator-escalation misclassifies `action_required` | Filed, unfixed |
| C50 | Stuck picker — the daemon serves a stale READY set | Filed, unfixed |

**A number collision, recorded rather than quietly renumbered.** `C45` was used the same day for two
different defects, and **both usages are anchored**, so neither can be dropped silently. The doorbell
owns it in the code (a `C45` comment block in `tmux_adapter.py`, plus `DOORBELL_MIGRATION.md` and two
test files); the `agent_audit.log` untrack (`73998d70`) owns it in `a70dbe1a`'s `.gitignore`
rationale, which cites *"C45 precedent — the same untrack-and-gitignore ruling already applied to
logs/agent_audit.log"*. The C-series is `mainD`'s namespace, so the renumber is filed to its owner
rather than settled here; in this record the second item is referred to by description and hash,
never by a number.

**C49, measured rather than asserted.** The daemon escalated **11 operator items**; a full parse of
the same 17-item standing queue found **zero** that need the human. The formula is reproducible
exactly: every item with `action_required: true` (12) minus `kind: status` (1) = 11. That formula
reads `action_required` as *"the operator must act"* when on this bus it means *"some named agent
must act next"* — and every one of the 11 is addressed to `auditor`, a main, or the coordinator in
its own text. Only one carries `kind: decision-request`, and its author disclaims it. Measured across
the live inboxes at wrap-up: **111 rows carry `action_required`, 8 satisfy the daemon's own
`_is_operator_item`, 7 overlap** — the two predicates are nearly disjoint, which is the defect.
An escalation that fires on a well-run night trains everyone to ignore it.

### The bus wipe

**The attribution the coordinator gave this all morning was wrong, and the corrected version is
worse, not better.** `a70dbe1a` did not delete anything: `git rm --cached` cannot delete an untracked
file. `mainD` established the deleter by scope — the sweep also took `cache/`, `iq2_arch_eval.py`,
`q4_architect_verdicts.json`, `opencode.json`, `artifacts/operator/unblock-receipts/`, two logs, and
all 29 nested worktrees (which require the *second* `f`) — as **`git clean -ffdx` run in
`/workspace`**. Untracked entries went 149 → 3, total dirty 232 → 56.

`a70dbe1a` is exonerated as the deleter and **load-bearing as the enabler**: its other half added
`.gitignore` entries for `cursors/`, `heartbeats/`, `inbox/`, `outbox/`, `claims/`, `advisory*.jsonl`
and `logs/bus_supervisor.pid`, converting the bus runtime from *tracked — and therefore immune to
`git clean` at any force level* — into ignored, and so `-x`-eligible. Reporting only the exoneration
understates how the two halves combined.

Damage: inbox **826 → 4** rows; `outbox/inference.jsonl` **85 → 2**; `outbox/mainD.jsonl` gone; all
cursors gone. At the ledger's measurement the bus could see **1 of 8 agents**: heartbeats 4/8,
cursors 1/8, outbox 3/8, `stuck_state.json` unreadable for everyone but `coordinator-agent`.

Archived to `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/bus-incident-20260812T0825Z/` — verified at wrap-up as a live
snapshot (72 files, 944 KB) plus 89 files / 2.7 MB recovered from `a70dbe1a^`. Held **outside**
`/workspace`, applying the finding: a scratch dir inside the repo is untracked and would be swept by
the very command being diagnosed.

**Deliberately NOT restored over the runtime.** 826 stale messages replayed against a zero cursor
would have recreated a mass re-drain — the C34 flood, by a different door. The archive is the record;
the runtime starts clean.

Three things follow, and all three are structural:

- **`git status` can never report a loss like this again**, because those paths are now ignored. The
  ordinary signal that something is missing is gone by construction.
- **`mainC`'s and `mainD`'s overnight outbox reports are unrecoverable.** Their committed blobs at
  `a70dbe1a^` were already 0 lines — the content was never committed and is now off disk. `mainA`
  (35 lines) and `auditor` (137) were recoverable.
- **The advisory history went with it.** The earliest surviving record is 08:20:31Z, which is
  precisely why the 4,602 figure above cannot be re-derived and 811 is what remains. The rotation
  that C38 correctly built shards to `advisory_<n>.jsonl` — inside the repo, and gitignored. A
  history that only exists in `clean -x` fodder is not a history.

> **The moment a path becomes ignored it becomes `clean -x` fodder.** Untracking live runtime should
> move it *out* of the repo, not merely hide it *from* git. This is the second instance in twelve
> hours of converting *merge safety* into *clean exposure* — the `agent_audit.log` untrack is the
> other.

### Coordination mechanics — what actually worked

Two findings about the coordination itself, which is the only thing in this lane that is genuinely
the coordinator's own subject matter.

**A commit/push freeze was necessary to land a merge at all.** The reconciliation went stale eight
times against a five-writer tree; a 15-minute fleet-wide freeze is what let it land. A merge on a
shared tree is not slow because merging is hard — it is slow because the base moves, and the only
lever on that is stopping the writers, briefly and announced.

**Per-owner conflict resolution failed repeatedly; one agent absorbing the churn succeeded.** Asking
each owner to resolve their own paths sounds like the correct single-writer discipline and is the
wrong shape here: it serialises N round-trips against a base that keeps moving, so every resolution
is stale before the next arrives. Concentrating the churn in one agent converts N moving targets into
one. The single-writer rule governs *authorship*, not *reconciliation* — and conflating the two cost
most of a night.

## Research/eval backlog lane — `mainB` subagent (screened dispatch, 4 rows closed)

Worked `handoffs/active/research-evaluation-index.md` under a screen-before-you-work rule. **12 rows
screened with `backlog_row_check.py`; all 12 returned DISPATCHABLE.** That number is the finding: the
screener answers *is this row well-formed*, and it cannot answer *is this row still true*. Verifying
each premise against the world turned 3 of the 12 into already-done and 1 into dissolved — the same
1-in-6 hit rate measured on 2026-07-29, arrived at through a completely different failure mode
(stale-but-well-formed rather than anchor rot).

**Closed with re-derived evidence** (epyc-root `654bce6c`):

  as research `9cc8db2d`, filed under its OTHER tracking id (`ID-7`, closed in
  `intake-derived-work-2026-07-25.md`) while these three rows stayed open. **One piece of work, two
  trackers, one of them never ticked** — worth naming as a class, because no checkbox audit can see it:
  both files are internally consistent. Re-derived by `git log -S` + 12/12 tests, not by trusting either.
- `intake-derived-work-2026-07-25.md` ID-35 — corrected 2026-07-29 in both owner handoffs
  (`c942728e`). The row's own line anchors had rotted to unrelated paragraphs; restated by text.
- `autopilot-sequential-allocation.md` SEQ-A — dissolved, matching its already-closed children.
  **Residual defect found while verifying and fixed** (orchestrator `bb821f9b`): `f2ad030e` corrected
  the SEQ-A block and its inline comment but left the MODULE DOCSTRING asserting *"56 of 393 … the
  label is STICKY"* as fact. The corrected code shipped under an uncorrected headline, and the
  docstring is where every later citation of 56/393 came from. Retracted in place, not deleted.

**New work — the harness-level answer-leakage audit** (`scoring-infra-standardization.md` 264/267/270),
the one row of the four that was genuinely undone.

- *Prompt assembly and the judge path are clean.* All 17 `_row_to_prompt` sites keep
  `expected`/`canonical_solution`/`test`/`scoring_config.test_code`/`metadata.bug_explanation` out of
  the prompt; `_generate_question` builds `call_kwargs` from the prompt plus routing flags only; the
  rubric judge is a separate `force_mode="direct"`, `allow_delegation=False` call gated on
  `check_cross_family`. No in-run grade channel.
- *The exposure is filesystem reach.* `REPLEnvironment.ALLOWED_FILE_PATHS` and the live `read_file`
  both resolve to `['/mnt/raid0/llm/', '/tmp/']`, and `question_pool.jsonl` — **79,479 rows, 78,341
  with a non-empty `expected`**, mode 0664 — sits inside it. The REPL surface advertised to the model
  offers `grep(pattern, file_path=...)` and `CALL(name, **kw)`. One grep of its own prompt returns its
  own answer. **Proven functionally** (`_validate_file_path(pool) → (True, None)`, `read_file(pool)`
  returns content), not inferred from reading. Exposure, not an observed exploit.
- *Second, distinct defect found while proving the first*: `orchestration/tools/system.py`
  `_is_path_allowed` gates on `os.path.abspath`, which does not resolve symlinks. A probe symlink under
  `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/` pointing at `/etc/passwd` passed the check and `read_file` returned it (probe
  removed). The REPL's own `_validate_file_path` uses `realpath` and refused the same path — **two gates
  sharing an allow-list but not its enforcement.**
- Remediation left as two costed options in the handoff rather than taken: the row authorised an audit,
  and both fixes touch a production read path. Corroboration that the threat model is already ours:
  `_register_eval_tools` mints eval secrets to a tmpfs path *"outside read_file's allowed roots, so the
  model-under-test cannot grep it from the repo"* — solved for one tool, unsolved for the 78,341-row pool.

Not taken, and why: `repl-turn-efficiency.md:120` (provenance fixes) requires editing intake entries,
which CLAUDE.md gates on explicit operator approval for sub-agents. `frontier-f4-continuity-backup.md`
W2 is genuinely operator-blocked — no off-host target is configured and `/workspace` and
`/mnt/raid0/llm` are the same array, so any backup written today would be the fake same-array one the
row forbids.

## Inference-research backlog lane — `mainB` subagent (57 rows screened, 5 closed, 0 compute)

Worked `handoffs/active/inference-research-index.md`, the largest domain, under the same
screen-before-you-work rule and under a hard **no-compute** constraint (no bench, no server, no GPU, no
region claim). Four commits, all pathspec-limited: `40b873e2`, `a3647dff`, `4225f308`, `cfa33cfe`.

**The headline is a missed gate, not a closed row.** `log-linear-gated-deltanet-readiness.md` had carried
"no pretrained checkpoint" since 2026-04-14, restated at the 2026-05-28 audit reset and again at the
2026-07-26 staleness review. The paper author's own checkpoint — `hanguo/log-linear-attention`, public,
ungated, MIT — has been on HuggingFace since **2026-02-13**. All three gates fire. It was never upstream
that was slow; the monthly HF row in the handoff's own Monitoring Targets table was simply never run.
Identity proven from the artifact rather than the card: `model_type h_gated_deltanet` (the baseline sibling
in the same repo is `gated_deltanet`, and the two `configs/flame/*_mid.json` are otherwise identical), plus
two tensors standard GDN does not have — `attn.L [6, 15]` and `attn.l_proj [90, 1536]`, 90 = 6 heads ×
15 levels, 15 = ceil(log2 16384)+1. 795.690 M params, f32, read out of the safetensors header.

Three things found while proving it, each of which changes what an executor would do:

- **The reference is not runnable as shipped.** `forward()` wires only the Triton chunk kernel
  (`else: raise NotImplementedError`), and `make_levels_matrix` `torch.load`s
  `/export/share/experiments/...` — an author-cluster path in no repo. Both fixable, both invisible until
  you read the code. The pure-PyTorch `hattention/recurrent.py` needs neither and is the port reference.
- **A claim in the handoff was backwards.** *"State size 4-10x reduction … at 262K context"* sits under a
  paragraph framing this as the replacement for standard GDN. On the released dims the log-linear state is
  the standard state × 15 levels: **557 MB vs 37 MB over 21 layers, a 15× increase**. The reduction is
  against softmax KV cache, which the bullet never named. Corrected with the arithmetic in place.
- The released architecture caps at 16384 positions with a fixed 15-level set, so "at 262K" does not
  qualify it at all.

**Two rows resolved by reading the frozen kernel, not by running it** (`llamacpp-v6-consolidation.md`,
NEEDS-OPERATOR-REVIEW). Both were "port or drop?" questions; both answers are *drop*, for better reasons
than "superseded":

- SWA slot-reuse `d1c72d7fc`/`603702769` did not **add** SWA reuse — `d1c72d7fc` **deleted** upstream's
  `cells.seq_pos_max(seq_get(idx)) + 1` test and substituted the incoming batch's position, dropping the
  `seq_get(idx)` lookup. Under the enclosing `seq_count(idx) == 1` guard the owner may be a *different*
  sequence, so one slot's cells get judged against another slot's positions. The commit's own validation
  was single-sequence Gemma-3 spec-decode, which cannot reach that path. v9 carries the original upstream
  form verbatim.
- `--moe-n-expert` (`86901388a`) needs no patch at all: `--override-kv <arch>.expert_used_count=int:N` is
  numerically equivalent, because `ggml_argsort_top_k` is a DESC argsort plus a view, so the first N of the
  top-M *are* the top-N — the fork just pays two extra `ggml_cont` copies per MoE layer. Its
  expert-count/quality data (excellent at 50%, gibberish at 12.5%) was preserved as observation-grade
  rather than dropped with the code.

**A premise defect of the kind that survives every checkbox audit.** `angelslim-techniques-evaluation.md`
blocked on *"PR #22836 (AngleSlim kernels)"*. The number is right; the parenthetical is wrong — #22836 is
*"ggml-cpu: add STQ1_0 ternary quantization with **ARM NEON** vec_dot kernel"*, still open. No llama.cpp PR
adds AngelSlim kernels (`angleslim`, the row's spelling, returns 0 hits; AngelSlim produces checkpoints,
not kernels). The load-bearing part: its 17 files touch `arch/arm/quants.c` and `arch-fallback.h` and **no
`arch/x86/`**, so a merge gives Zen 5 the scalar fallback. `tq3-quantization-evaluation.md` owns the merge
watch and its bench row said "when STQ1_0 lands, llama-bench …" — that bench would have measured a scalar
path and read as *"Sherry does not transfer to EPYC"*. Trigger re-scoped to **merged AND has an x86
vec_dot**; the CUDA sibling #23332 was closed unmerged. **A compute window saved by a read-only check.**

**Triage of the whole index.** 57 distinct rows screened across the index's 53 handoffs — a first-open-task
sweep (48 rows; the other 6 link targets have no open box) plus 9 targeted screens. 2 came back NOT
DISPATCHABLE (`engram:377`, `intra-process-tensor-parallel-decode:34`), both correctly refused under
standing-constraint guards. **5 handoffs have no open task at all** — `01-speculative-decoding`,
`fable5-window2-findings-05b`, `moe-aggregate-deployment-wins-brief`, `tree-draft-forward-port-plan`,
`v7-promotion`. Their index next-actions are all *retire / extract to docs / move to completed*. The only
reason this lane did not close them is that finishing one deletes an index row, which this subagent was not
authorised to do. **They are the five cheapest items in the domain and they need one authorised session, not
a research window.**

That leaves 45 dispatchable rows (48 swept − 2 guarded − 1 that is the row this lane itself opened). Split by

**Method note worth keeping.** `git merge-base --is-ancestor` exits non-zero both when the answer is *no*
and when the command **errors** — and it errored silently here, because zsh parses `$V9:src/file.cpp` as a
history modifier and `V9` was unset in that invocation. Every ancestry claim above was re-run with literal
hashes plus two positive controls (v8 → v9, and v9 → itself, both exit 0). Catalogue face 8 with a
different laundering mechanism; the tell was three consecutive `NOT-ancestor` results next to an
`echo`-only output block.

## AutoKernel governed raw-HIP arm closure

The smallest real hand-HIP loop now runs end to end without touching either frozen production or an
experimental llama.cpp tree. Research `2c214d48` `controller/hip_authoring_arm.py` accepts only an exact
`torch2hip/<suite>/<task>` from clean Apache-2.0 AgentKernelArena commit `2dbbf1d3`, hashes every
vendor task file plus the candidate and evaluator/Ninja/hipcc identities, binds the physical MI210
as gfx90a, and compiles while GPU-blind. It acquires separate device claims only around the vendor
PyTorch baseline and centralized final correctness/timing evaluation. TERM/INT cleanup is armed and
the acquisition assignment race is signal-deferred.

The final r4 proof used `torch2hip/gpumode/16636_SiLU`, compiled for gfx90a, and passed **11/11 public
correctness cases and 11/11 timing-validity cases**. Claims `akd-d61fe36073ec4ccd` and
`akd-7cc6b1670eb84bed` were separately acquired and released; samplers recorded 11 and 222 samples.
Receipt:
`/mnt/raid0/llm/autokernel/campaigns/hip-arm-silu-roundtrip-20260812-r4/receipt.json`, internal SHA-256
`1cb7087f715a2a9ac28b187a3f2d25c41be6a82279ca4fb254ac9b481805bc48`, file SHA-256
`e682ca027781acc03e4ce33ef8584ec9660721711aadd10670791ddfbbe5fc89`. The root Vidya reader
independently re-derived both producer-authored rows from that exact file. The observed Torch-eager
ratio (1.9082) is deliberately non-rankable: shapes are public and an honest vendor baseline is not
bound. Decision-grade HIP ranking still requires the explicit C2/C6 sealed/unseen hardening and C3
vendor-baseline gate; this closure claims compatibility and instrumentation only.

During initial activation the pinned evaluator environment was missing its declared Ninja runtime.
The isolated `/mnt/raid0/llm/tools/geak-v1-rocm62-py312` environment now contains exact
`ninja==1.13.0`; r4 binds the environment-local executable and digest. After release the MI210 showed
0% use, 0% VRAM, no KFD processes, and both device lockfiles were empty. No session-wide compute
claim remains.

## AutoKernel ROCm-provider boundary

The operator accepted a durable scope clarification for closing the NVIDIA/CUDA efficiency gap:
AutoKernel should search the software-addressable ROCm layer, but ROCm modules remain governed
candidate providers rather than new champion source trees. The current local evidence makes the
distinction necessary rather than cosmetic. Dense FP16 reaches 62.6% of spec bandwidth while local
vLLM-ROCm reaches 69.2%; exact-shape hipBLASLt/rocBLAS ratios span roughly 0.73x–1.32x, so provider
selection is material and shape-specific. By contrast, the utilization collapse to 21.3% for MoE Q8
and 10.3% for MoE IQ2 points primarily at custom low-bit/layout/gather/dispatch paths rather than an
ordinary dense GEMM library. The eight-arm flash-attention/rocWMMA/MMQ-MFMA experiment also spans
about 18.7k–24.6k t/s on one surface, showing that integration and build policy can be material
without a vendor-runtime rewrite.

Root now records this as AK-D39 and in the system-wide design: AutoKernel may tune exact-shape
rocBLAS/hipBLASLt/CK choice, algorithms, compiler flags, launch topology, standalone Triton/HIP
kernels, and isolated source-available module forks. It may not mutate shared `/opt/rocm`, replace a
resident runtime, or treat an opaque vendor binary as authored champion source. A winning module
result becomes bankable only through an experimental `llama_gpu` integration with exact operator,
captured-workload, and whole-model evidence; the champion remains the deployable `llama.cpp`
lineage. A separately deployed module would require a future human-reviewed backend and release
adapter rather than inferred authority.

## AutoKernel pre-reboot fault-rehearsal repair

The no-inference pre-reboot regression sweep found a real child-process launch defect rather than an
empirical blocker. `fault_rehearsal.py` imported successfully in the parent because unittest/pytest
had inserted `scripts/kernel_rnd` into in-process `sys.path`, but its fresh crash/restart and claim-
holder children inherited no equivalent `PYTHONPATH`. Both exited before publishing their ready
markers. The validator test then missed its intended status-derivation mutation only because the
underlying rehearsal receipt had already failed.

Research main `eeaf80ba` makes the child environment self-contained: the resolved package parent for
the exact checkout is placed first in `PYTHONPATH`, ahead of any inherited entries. The receipt still
binds the source commit and producer digest, and no process other than the exact captured children is
selected or signalled. The smoke test also now accepts a detached clean checkout by its required full
commit identity instead of treating a branch label as provenance. Focused fault/placement coverage
passes **12/12**; the complete AutoKernel suite passes **5,548**, with one expected failure and
**2,241 subtests**. This repair ran no build, benchmark, inference, GPU workload, stack action, or
production mutation.

## AutoKernel AK-D39 executable provider boundary

AK-D39 is now enforced in research main rather than existing only as a governance decision. Research
commit `77689f767fc16bc598a64a81e97dec9fa537f0f6` advances the current proposal contract to v4 with a
versioned `provider_reference`: provider kind, source-versus-opaque mode, immutable source/artifact
identity, licence check, isolated prefix, toolchain and linkage manifests, target backend, and evidence
authority. Historical proposal v2/v3 records remain readable but cannot acquire provider authority by
relabelling.

The implementation closes four laundering routes:

- opaque provider binaries are diagnostic-only and cannot be banked;
- provider prefixes are realpath-resolved and refused both beneath and above `/opt/rocm`, `/usr`, or
  any frozen production kernel tree, including symlink aliases and overly broad roots such as `/opt`
  or `/mnt/raid0/llm`;
- rocBLAS/hipBLASLt baseline observations carry exact versioned provider manifests instead of a bare
  provider label; and
- C3's diagnostic Apex/standalone-provider binding cannot satisfy the whole-model exit. Only a clean,
  production-descended experimental `llama.cpp`/`llama_gpu` commit with bound patch, binary, linkage,
  toolchain, and isolation identities can do so. Candidate and champion admission re-check the same
  provider/integration join.

Verification after rebasing onto research main `58b8d66a` passed the 580-test focused and affected
suite. Full discovery reached 5,597 tests and exposed four current-schema call sites in the newly
landed least-commitment path; those were repaired before the final commit and the 419-test affected
slice passed. The only remaining discovery error was environmental: the checkout's Python does not
have `pytest`, required by `test_placement_context.py`. No inference, benchmark, GPU workload,
production-tree mutation, or stack action ran during this checkpoint.

No new AutoKernel task is filed for that import error: the absent package is a property of this
session's system-Python test environment, not an AK-D39 product gap, and every AK-D39 plus affected
current-schema test ran successfully. The empirical IQK and EPYC-capture tasks remain filed in their
existing owning checklists rather than being duplicated here.

## AutoKernel raw-HIP decision-grade hardening (10:02–10:22 UTC)

The minimum raw-HIP compatibility arm could compile and pass the public AgentKernelArena SiLU cases,
but it could not be ranked: public shapes were visible, Torch eager was not an honest compiled vendor
floor, and candidate execution lacked a sealed hostile oracle boundary. Research commit `b8d8c409`
adds the task-local decision seam without touching or building frozen production. Candidate source is
sealed before a cryptographic suite seed materializes 24 hostile boundary cases; expected outputs stay
in an independent host-double parent; a C6-contained child receives inputs but never expected values;
and two differently poisoned outputs must be fully overwritten, preserve input, and repeat bitwise.
The C3 comparison uses `torch.compile(..., backend="inductor", fullgraph=True, dynamic=False)` over the
exact SiLU expression on the same tensor and MI210, with the generated provider implementation tree
hash-bound in the receipt.

The first complete timing attempt, r4, exposed a measurement defect during independent review: its 200
launches produced about 2 ms per arm/block, far below RVP-C3-5's locally measured 250,090,903 ns floor.
r4 is therefore superseded instrument evidence and must not be described as decision-grade. The runner
now records total arm duration and calls the existing gfx90a admission instrument separately for every
candidate/provider arm. The terminal r6 campaign used 30,000 launches per arm/block. It passed 24/24
sealed cases (maximum absolute error `1.2280521e-6`), all 40 arm windows passed the duration floor
(minimum `276,039,581 ns`), and all C6 static/sandbox/cgroup checks were clean. Across 20 randomized
paired blocks, raw HIP measured a `1.076934974x` median speedup over exact Torch-ROCm-compile with MAD
relative effect `0.004371602`; all 20 block signs were positive and the anytime-valid sign martingale
crossed threshold 20 at block 9. Internal receipt SHA-256 is
`0ba60558951d47b596abce52ebe41401ae50a1326375595c49dfaf737f902ccb`; file SHA-256 is
`c2b7f7ce983ef31ea92d6977450e993c2ae0f4fdf07db595de26caf5341174ab`.

Authority remains deliberately narrow: the receipt is rankable only against the exact task-local
provider and explicitly carries no release or promotion authority. Any future production proposal still
requires integration into a fresh experimental llama candidate and whole-model/operator gates under
AK-D39. After deliberate replay onto research main `948a95a4`, focused validation passed 23/23 and the
full AutoKernel tree passed 5,608 tests with one expected xfail and 2,344 subtests. Research main now
contains the combined AK-D39 and HIP implementation at
`9609b686e7932e2c0673b61a764fa9853834197d`. Root SC35 extends the strict Vidya adapter and proves
that r6's two producer-authored rows project as Witnessed/Attested while sub-floor timing or invented
release authority fails closed.

## AutoKernel INF-03 r4 integrity retraction and dashboard correction

The live INF-03 audit found a cross-cutting defect after the previous checkpoint had classified the
campaign as merely incomplete. `ArenaWorkspaceEvaluator` evaluated every intermediate KernelFoundry
candidate, but the controller process was deliberately GPU-blind and held no MI210 claim. The r4
KernelFoundry controller receipt records **64 intermediate evaluations**, 64 model calls, and 60 QD
transitions; its evaluator log records **64/64 centralized correctness failures**. Those evaluator
actions ran outside the only two durable vendor/final measurement windows. Consequently the search
feedback was invalid even though the final centralized checkpoint itself was structurally complete.

The owning session terminated only its captured campaign PID chain and confirmed all three PIDs dead.
The last vendor/final claim had already released, and no device claim remained. The partial campaign is
preserved unchanged at
`/mnt/raid0/llm/autokernel/campaigns/inf03-available-source-six-arm-20260812-r4`, with manifest
self-hash `941fece21400362f41772682ec2cf8e3162b08488183a767dce276209ec61eaf`. Its immutable inventory is
**5/64 checkpoints**, **2/24 cells**, **10/10 released** durable measurement windows, 215 numeric
samples, and 52.588 claimed GPU-seconds. It has no aggregate and is now explicitly stopped,
non-resumable, non-rankable, and invalid for controller comparison; none of its partial observations
may be banked or reused in a fresh attempt.

The accepted repair boundary has two layers:

1. A parent-worker-owned AF_UNIX broker must authenticate the exact spawned controller PID with
   `SO_PEERCRED`, accept bounded candidate bytes, evaluate them in fresh workspaces, and acquire/release
   one governed MI210 claim per intermediate evaluator action. Controller and evaluated candidate
   processes receive neither broker authority nor claim credentials.
2. Exact-PID brokerage fixes resource ownership but does not defeat a same-UID controller or candidate
   reading `/proc` or attacking shared process/workspace state. A **decision-bearing** live pilot also
   requires controller/candidate OS isolation. Until that boundary is proven, a broker exercise is an
   engineering smoke only: it cannot choose, eliminate, rank, bank, or advance a controller policy.

The Kernel-R&D hub now applies a versioned, exact-manifest retraction from
`dashboard/arena_attempt_dispositions.json`. It keeps r4's historical counts visible while rendering
`STOPPED · DIAGNOSTIC HISTORY ONLY`, `campaign evidence invalid`, `resume forbidden`, and `not
rankable`. The overlay is deliberately one-way and cannot admit evidence or improve producer health.
INF-03's thin index action now directs implementers to repair brokerage and OS isolation, then run a
fresh one-task/one-arm pilot before spending another panel.

Derived-actionable sweep: three tasks were filed in the owning handoff—the exact-PID broker, the OS
isolation gate, and the fresh pilot/campaign sequence. No new handoff or duplicate index owner was
created. Explicit decline: r4 is retained as diagnostic history rather than deleted, but it will not be
resumed or treated as regression evidence for controller quality.
## Governance lane — 2026-08-12 lessons converted to durable repo policy (`22c4aff5`)

Operator directive: *"MEMORY FILES ARE POINTLESS. DURABLE AGENT KNOWLEDGE/POLICY is WHAT MAKES YOU
ROBUST."* Seven lessons a coordinator had been recording in a private per-user memory store are now
agent files that the whole fleet inherits and a reviewer can audit.

**Placement follows the auto-load finding**: `AGENTS.md` is a symlink to `CLAUDE.md`, and `CLAUDE.md`
is the only file a main auto-loads — no `@`-imports, no `SessionStart` hook. So each rule is canonical
in exactly ONE file, with a short digest plus anchor link in `CLAUDE.md` where it must bind at startup.

| Lesson | Canonical home | Digest |
|---|---|---|
| Three liveness states (working / **compacting** / idle); the pane is not the instrument; an adapter refusal citing runtime state is a finding, not an obstacle | `agents/shared/SESSION_LIFECYCLE.md` → *Reading another session's liveness* | CLAUDE.md § Agents & Automation; coordinator guardrail; escalation-ladder step 1 rewritten |
| Heartbeats lie (mainB: `working` while 446 s stale, pane idle 3 cycles, GPU 0%); hardware wins a three-way disagreement | same section | same |
| Sample DURING, not after — a post-exit sample cannot separate *never resident* from *finished* | `agents/shared/OPERATING_CONSTRAINTS.md` → *Observation Windows* | CLAUDE.md § Debugging |
| Idle compute needs persistence across samples; `llama-bench` exits between probes; queue back-to-back | same section (+ saturation clause) | same |
| Line number is a hint, task TEXT is the identity | `OPERATING_CONSTRAINTS.md` → *Dispatching Backlog Work* | CLAUDE.md § Handoff Workflow; coordinator guardrail |
| A screener proves WELL-FORMED, not STILL-NEEDED (4 of 8 screened rows already satisfied) | same section | same |
| `ldd` cannot prove a HIP run — llama.cpp *dlopens* `libggml-hip.so` | `OPERATING_CONSTRAINTS.md` § Inference and Benchmarks | CLAUDE.md § Debugging |

Four incident narratives filed: `INC-20260812-compacting-read-as-idle`, `INC-20260812-post-exit-vram-sample`,
`INC-20260812-dispatch-by-line-number`, and `INC-20260731-ggml-linkage-silent-cpu-fallback` — the last cited
by the wiki and by epyc-inference-research `7f310022` but never filed until now.

**Two findings from doing the work.** (1) `MEASUREMENT_POLICY.md` was the natural home for the sampling
rules and is human-amendment-only; the trust-boundary hook refused the edit, so they landed beside

carrying every digest anchor was the one class of governance link nothing checked. Now in scope, and

## Observation contract — a durable measure for the watchdog-blindness class

**Specimen.** `bus_supervisor.sh` identified the coordinator-daemon with
`pgrep -f "session_bus_coordinator\.py run"`. The live daemon's argv carried `--bus-root <path>`
between `.py` and `run`, so the pattern matched nothing. The watchdog declared a **healthy, actively
heartbeating** daemon dead — forever — and relaunch-looped every ~10s until somebody happened to read
its log. Root cause is not the regex: the guard could not observe the thing it guards and **nothing
detected that**. It held two signals, required BOTH, and the working one was overruled by the broken
one. The instance repair was owned by another session; this entry is the **class**.

**Why a lint could not be the measure.** The supervisor's own launch line agreed with its own pattern.
Reality did not, because the live daemon had been started by a different caller with an extra flag.
Nothing in the file was wrong when read — the mismatch existed only at runtime. So the measure is a
runtime one, with a fast static half at commit time.

**Built.**
- `scripts/coordination/observer_guard.sh` — three-valued observation: `present` / `absent` /
  **`unobservable`**, the state the specimen lacked. Two independent detectors: cross-channel
  disagreement, and a **blind streak** where *launch is the positive control* (a watchdog that has
  relaunched its target N times and still never seen it has evidence about its own eyes). Corrective
  action is licensed only by `absent`; blind is loud — greppable `OBSERVER-BLIND` banner, a
  machine-readable breadcrumb, and `observer_guard.sh alerts` exiting 3 for any poller.
- `scripts/coordination/observer_registry.json` + `observer_census.py` — the census, and the two
  **discovery rules** that make it survive its author: any file identifying a process by name/argv,
  or sourcing the guard, enrols itself. Deferrals are legal and must be **bound to a real unchecked
  `- [ ]` task**; checking it off or deleting it without the migration turns the suite red.
- `tests/test_observer_contract.py` (29 tests) — the behavioural battery drives each adopted
  watchdog's uniform `observe` entrypoint against a real stand-in process through channel
  disagreement and partial blindness, and asserts action is SUPPRESSED while blind and still TAKEN
  when the target is genuinely absent.
- `scripts/hooks/observer_census_precommit.sh` + `pre-commit.extras` — stdlib-only, 0.09s, wired live.
- `backfill_supervisor.sh` migrated as the reference adoption (it was the specimen's exact twin, and
  it `kill -9`'d every pid a pattern returned on a shared host).

**Ten instances filed** as OBS-1..OBS-10 across three owning handoffs. Highest severity: OBS-3,
`nightshift/inference_guard.sh` fails **open** — every failure mode yields 0 GB, prints "No heavy
inference detected", and lets the full agent workload launch on top of a live 200 GB+ inference run.

**Findings from doing the work.** (1) The battery caught a real bug in the reference adoption: under
`set -euo pipefail` a command substitution that exits non-zero aborts the script, and `og_verdict`
exits 1/3 *by design* — so the three-state verdict was silently killing the supervisor. (2) `og_proc_scan`
initially reported `present` for a string that existed nowhere but in the scanning shell's own command
line: CLAUDE.md's "a guard's argv necessarily contains the names it guards", reproduced by the guard
itself. (3) Discovery via `git ls-files` alone was vacuous for new files — mutation-testing Rule A
against an untracked watchdog left it green until untracked-not-ignored files were included.

## fleet_watch.sh productionised — the coordinator's stall detector, rebuilt and tested

`scripts/coordination/fleet_watch.sh` was hand-written by the coordinator on its own thread under
time pressure, ran uncommitted as pid 4044227, and had never been tested. Rebuilt, tested, swapped
in (old pid confirmed dead, new pid 1448583 running), and committed.

**Defects found in the prototype, all of them silent.** `findings` were emitted through
`printf '%b'`, so composer text containing a backslash escape was re-interpreted on the way to the
log and the literal `GPU 0%%` rendered as `0%%` — visible in the old log's last lines. An unreadable
`rocm-smi` collapsed to `${gpu:-0}` = "0%", i.e. **a missing GPU tool would have manufactured a
permanent COMPUTE-IDLE alarm**. A vanished tmux session `continue`d past every pane and logged a
clean "ok — no stalls" forever — the biggest possible finding rendered as silence. `regions_free`
was `grep -c 'free'` over whole lines, which counts any lock holder whose command line contains the
word, against a hardcoded 4. The composer was located by taking the LAST prompt-glyph match anywhere
in the pane, so a transcript line answers for an empty composer — and transcript text never changes,
so it satisfies the persistence rule and alarms forever. Per-agent state used
`printf -v "prev_${agent}"`, which is not a valid assignment for a roster id containing a hyphen
(`coordinator-agent`). No log rotation, no single-instance guard, no config validation (a
non-numeric `INTERVAL` makes `sleep` fail instantly and busy-loops a core on a shared host).

**The first-cycle lie, fixed.** On cycle 1 the prototype logged "ok — no stalls, compute in use"
even when compute was idle, because persistence had not accumulated — health asserted from an
absence of evidence it had not had time to collect. "Not yet determined" is now a distinct verdict.
Side by side in the log across the swap, on an unchanged fleet: old `ok — no stalls, no pending
input, compute in use` vs new `warming — cycle 1/3, persistence not yet accumulated; NOTHING is
asserted yet (compute=idle, mains determined 6/6)`.

**Authoritative sources first.** `tmux_adapter.py probe --json` (read-only; ledger byte-identical
across a full six-main sweep) supplies `runtime_state`, `window_quiet_for_s` and `pane_dead`; the
composer row is located by tmux's own `cursor_y`; pane glyphs are last-resort corroboration. One
trap worth recording: **`probe --json` exits EX_BLOCKED — non-zero — for any main that is merely
busy**, while printing complete valid JSON, so judging the probe by its exit status would discard
the authoritative signal on exactly the mains that have one. The verdict is taken from whether the
JSON parses. Measured coverage: only `inference` (Codex) returns a runtime state; the five Claude
mains return `None` ("no runtime signal implemented yet"), so their idle reports stay CANDIDATES.

**New: DETECTOR-BLIND.** Every vendor TUI string is gathered in one named block, and if no readable
pane matches ANY known marker for PERSIST_CYCLES the detector reports that its vocabulary has
drifted and SUPPRESSES the idle verdicts built on it — six mains losing their markers in the same
cycle is a TUI release, not a fleet-wide stall. This is the canary OBS-9 asks for.

**Evidence.** `tests/test_fleet_watch.sh` — 76 assertions, every rule exercised in BOTH directions,
with the compliant paths (text that changes each cycle, a submitted composer, a busy marker vetoing
a quiet window, the inter-probe 0%/0% gaps inside a healthy `llama-bench` sweep) asserted NOT to
fire. `tests/test_fleet_watch_mutation.sh` — 21 deliberate breakages, **21 caught, 0 survived**,
plus a control case requiring the pristine script to pass first.

**Findings from doing the work.** (1) `findings=$(fw_run_cycle)` runs the cycle in a SUBSHELL and
discards every persistence counter, so no condition could ever reach its threshold: the detector
would run forever, report nothing, and look healthy. Caught in my own draft before it shipped; the
same trap then reappeared in the test harness and made 8 cases pass for the wrong reason. (2) Five
of seventeen `sed`-based mutations silently failed to apply — the harness now requires each mutation
to match EXACTLY once and be proven applied, so a rotted pattern is reported as a defect rather than
as a pass. (3) One mutation survived and was CORRECT to survive: `floor(q) >= t` is equivalent to
`q >= t` for an integer threshold, so it was an equivalent mutant, not a hole. (4) The suite's own
guard-check grep had lost a brace and matched nothing — it had only ever been run against the
script it was written to reject, so it looked fine while refusing everything. A check exercised in
one direction only is not a check.

Residuals filed as OBS-11 (observation-contract adoption), FW-1 (the Codex empty composer renders a
placeholder that no available signal distinguishes from real queued text), FW-2 (no runtime liveness
for Claude mains) and FW-3 (nothing supervises the watcher) in
`handoffs/active/session-bus-thin-dispatcher.md`; `fleet_watch.sh` enrolled in
`observer_registry.json` and added to OBS-9's file list in `handoffs/active/non-inference-backlog.md`.

## Stranded `mainC` backlog — B5 / B11 / B13 executed by subagent (zero compute)

Three rows assigned to the unreachable `mainC` pane, run out-of-band. `mainC` did not report on
them; if it resumes and does, the overlap is this section.

**All three premises were wrong in the same direction — the row described a state the repo had
already moved past.** That is now 7 of 11 rows fact-checked today whose premise was stale, and the
screener cannot see any of it: `backlog_row_check.py` proves WELL-FORMED, never STILL-NEEDED. Two of
the three rows were also UNRESOLVABLE by exact text, so screening had to start from the owning
handoff.

- **B11 — "the ONE missing applicator".** `config_applicator.restart_role()` has existed since the
  2026-06-27…07-04 slices and the 2026-08-05 audit re-confirmed it. What was genuinely missing was
  the row's own second acceptance clause. Coverage-measured before writing anything: the
  `role == "orchestrator"` branch — the only consult of the API health gate — had **zero** executed
  statements across all 44 tests, because every one passes a MODEL role. The rollback tests that
  existed induce a *reload* or a *smoke* failure; both reach the same rollback block from a
  different source, so a health-gate regression was invisible. Five induced-failure tests, 44 -> 49
  counted, `restart_role()` now at zero missing statements, five mutations each caught by exactly
  one test. No reload executed — that belongs to the inference-owning session
  (INC-20260728-reload-preemption), so W3's live-attestation half is now written in blocker grammar
  and the screener refuses to re-dispatch it. Orchestrator `e462f752`.
- **B13 — "OFF purely because the dependency is declared nowhere".** The dependency really is
  undeclared (and really is absent from the venv), but that is not why reranking is off: rerank is
  gated off by **measured policy** (`KB_RAG_RERANK` default False, K7 "not default-safe"), so
  installing it changes nothing there. What the missing dep actually breaks is the
  **unconditional** first-stage KB-RAG encoder path. The fail-open is the bigger finding and is now
  filed with the family: three loaders swallow `ImportError` into one `logger.warning`, callers
  return their input unmodified, **nothing** asserts encoder availability anywhere in `src/api/`,
  and `federation.ensure_encoder_importable()`'s three-tier site-packages search exists purely to
  route around the undeclared dependency. Declared in the sole manifest with six counted guards
  incl. a positive control; **not installed** — `uv sync` against a live shared venv is the
  environment owner's call. Orchestrator `fa3daeac`.
- **B5 / EPD-3 — "the index mixes >=3 text conventions".** Already false: the `20260809T160329Z`
  reseed rebuilt 63,786/63,786 rows from the single canonical builder, and only 233 rows have been
  written since. **"Re-embed under one convention" has already been executed and the leak survived
  it.** Verified read-only on the live store: the leak moved from the FORMAT to a FIELD VALUE —
  `priority` takes exactly two values store-wide, `interactive` (34,938) and absent (29,081),
  splitting the writer paths at 99.973% purity, and it is *defaulted* by
  `task_ir.py:81` rather than observed, so it carries ~zero task semantics and ~total writer-path
  identity. It is also a **live** defect, not just a training confound: every live query embeds
  `priority:interactive` while 45.4% of the corpus does not.
  **Decision: one convention, with the convention corrected to drop `priority`. Partition-by-writer-path
  rejected** — it preserves the defect as a permanent schema fact, costs 29,081 rows of a corpus
  already shown to carry real decision signal, buys de-confounding that was already tested and
  found unnecessary (partial corr unchanged at −0.875), and does not even work as a post-filter
  because `retrieve_by_similarity` is a global KNN with `k*2` over-fetch.
  Found while gathering that evidence and fixed: the standing integrity gate re-implemented the
  embed-text convention instead of importing it, so on 54.6% of rows the check its own comment calls
  "the check internal consistency cannot fake" was cosine-ing each vector against a string that was
  never embedded. Orchestrator `604c70bf`, 11 -> 14 tests, two mutations caught. The recipe change
  itself is correctly operator-gated: dropping `priority` before the re-embed would take live-query
  mismatch from 45.4% to 100%.

Method notes worth keeping. (1) Mutation testing in a five-session shared clone was done by loading
an out-of-tree mutated **copy** of the module under the real module name, never by editing the
tracked file — a few seconds of a mutated tracked file is exactly the window the commit-sweep hazard
needs. (2) The B5 fix shipped a bug on the first run — a local named `record` shadowed the reporter
closure the same block calls in its `except` handler — and the *pre-existing* tests caught it. Rule
out the test method first: the new tests failing alongside three old ones was the tell that the
script, not the assertions, was broken.

## Pre-reboot staging lane — S-01/S-02/S-03 (`b92845c2`, `a2ca4143`; zero compute)

Executed the two highest-value pre-reboot staging tasks from
`handoffs/active/reboot-gated-inventory-and-staging.md`. Both landed; **both also falsified the
premise they were filed under**, which is the part worth keeping.

**S-01/S-02 — topology re-pin.** Live hash **`171f86f9188211e9`**, derived through the enforcing
consumers' own path (`preflight_gate._live_topology_hash()` →
`contention.topology_fingerprint_for_matrix(stack_numa.NUMA_CONFIG, matrix)`), never copied. Re-pinned
**36** entries across 7 files to that hash + `kernel_era: production-consolidated-v9`, and recompiled
`manifest.yaml` — the entries alone are not the consumer, the compiled manifest is, and it still
carried 41 stale occurrences after the entry edit. `compile_inference_batch.py validate` 52/52.
Deliverable is a **re-runnable deriver, not a number**: `scripts/coordination/derive_topology_hash.py`
hard-codes no hash and carries `--check-entries`, which refuses a vacuous empty-input pass. Recording a
number is precisely how three stale values (`8c8cfcbb13d2611d`, `df373c79cc4af06f`, `bc28e15d`) got
into circulation.

**The gate was failing OPEN, not closed.** The handoff's headline was that all 25 entries refuse today.
Measured through the real consumer: 21 of them **passed**. `run_batch_entry.topology_gate` takes its
"live" hash *from the B4 attestation* when the entry has no `topology_artifact` (none do), so a stale
pin and a stale attestation (`20260720T191355.json`, same dead hash) certified each other while both
disagreed with the host. The genuinely fail-closed consumer is the *other* one —
`preflight_gate.check_topology_hashes()`, invoked from inside `00-rcp-prologue.yaml`'s own command —
and that is what **mints** the attestation, so the stale pin was blocking its own repair. After the
re-pin the gate returns an honest `verified=false, "no matching B4 attestation"`. **Re-pinning was
necessary and is not sufficient**; a fresh attestation needs `--require-servers`, i.e. a live lineup,
and belongs to `inference` post-relaunch. 6/6 mutation assertions, run against a temp attestation dir
so no hand-written JSON could re-create the false pass.

**S-03 — first-boot host prep.** `scripts/session/host_prep.sh` covers the **eight** settings the gates
actually check, not the three named in the task. Also premise-falsifying: our `/` is an overlayfs and
the host's `/etc` is a different filesystem at `/proc/1/root/etc` (dev 45 vs 2050). The host **already**
persists `numa_balancing=0` twice — `99-epyc-inference.conf` overriding `20-numa.conf`'s `=1`, plus an
enabled `numa-balancing-off.service` — so the predicted post-reboot warning would never have fired.
Genuinely missing: `perf_event_paranoid` (reverts to 4), governor, THP `enabled`/`defrag`. Staged those
to boot (sysctl.d line + `epyc-host-prep.service`, enabled); applied nothing live.

Three method notes. (1) **The installer's first version would have written the container overlay** and
reported success — `/proc/1/root` is root-traversable only, so an unprivileged `[[ -d ]]` probe returned
false and the host-root resolution fell back to `/`. Its own `--verify-boot` caught it by claiming the
host did not persist `numa_balancing`, which we already knew to be false. It fails closed now; test H is
the regression. A verifier that can contradict its own installer is worth more than one that agrees.
(2) The live mutation was confined to `perf_event_paranoid` — a *permission* gate, not a performance
knob — gated on re-verified quiescence and restored by an `EXIT` trap. (3) Two of the three initial
suite failures were **test-method** artifacts (a plain file does not re-render THP brackets the way
sysfs does; root-owned fixtures need sudo to mutate) and one was a real `pipefail` hazard in my own
harness, where `health_check.sh | grep -q` returned health_check's exit code, not grep's.

**Observer contract.** The first revision reported live llama processes by argv match, tripping Rule A
and — because the census scans untracked files — blocking commits fleet-wide mid-edit. Fixed by
**deleting** the observation rather than enrolling it: nothing branched on it, a better detector already
owns the question (`find_llama_processes()` reads `/proc/<pid>/exe`), and it was fail-open in the
contract's exact specimen shape, with a genuine zero and a broken probe both printing `0`. Census back
to **14 observers, unchanged**. Second-order: the replacement *comment* re-triggered the census by
quoting the idiom verbatim — discovery is a source-pattern match, so the comment now describes the
command instead of reproducing it.

**Flagged, not fixed.** `max_uptime_days` has **zero code consumers** — schema-declared, never read by
any `.py`/`.sh` in the three repos. The only uptime enforcement is the hard-coded 7 d ceiling in
`server_np_sweep.py`. So the seven `max_uptime_days: 14` entries "expiring at 13:36Z" were never
reachable as decision-grade regardless of the pin, and the field is inert documentation wearing the
syntax of a gate. Routed to the owner in the handoff's §10.5 rather than patched from this lane.

blocker ("E5 Stage-B exclusively owns CPU") was stale, gate lifted on evidence (host load 2.07,
campaign closed). Results, auditor-recounted from the raw 401-row JSONL: **Gate (a) decisive** —
exactly 256 tensors carry real TC deltas (the byte-level LoRA prior, hit with zero threshold
tuning), 140/145 comparable non-LoRA tensors bit-identical, max residual 2.7e-9 RMS/elem, ~3,150×
separation → GGUF delta-geometry IS trustworthy at Q8. **Gate (b)** — cos(L) ≤0.011 flat across
all 64 layers: TC and FF are independent edits off stock. p(L) sits at ~0.45 NOT because of
lineage but because p=cos·r and r=43–145× (FF's edit dwarfs TC's LoRA): a detector artifact now
documented — cos(L) is the discriminator, p(L) never standalone. **Provenance surprise**: TC's
tensor topology (15 extra `blk.64.*`) matches the MTP checkpoint, not plain stock. Two instrument
defects found+fixed on first real execution (named-exclusion buckets replacing a silent-drop count
— 97 type-mismatched tensors incl. FF's BF16 `output.weight`; a broadcasting bug attempting a
112 TiB alloc that single-block synthetic chunks had hidden), +3 regression tests, 6/6 green.
Observation-grade only; authorizes no model/role/lineup action. ⚠ Research repo is mid-cherry-pick
(`9c29064b` autokernel, UU `FOOTPRINT.md`, not ours) so BOTH auditor lanes' research edits are
parked as patches at `/workspace/tmp/park-20260812/` + untracked snapshot; routed to coordinator

advanced to `fd46b520`); both auditor research halves patch-id-verified byte-identical to the
parked copies, re-tested green (6/6 unittest, 24/24 pytest), and landed — geometry `f640286a`,

## EffiBench-X canonical acceptance gate PASSED — with a fourth, runtime-discovered defect (auditor lane)

Row :597's premise held and paid out: run the instrument's own canonical solutions before any
model arm. 308 DATED Python problems × 2 canonical arms × 100 tests = 61,600 executions in ~10 min
(10 cpuset-pinned docker workers, cores 0–9, niced). **Verdict: FIT**, with conditions now recorded
in the handoff row: 306/308 pass the harness's own any-canonical rule; exclude `leetcode_3264` +
`leetcode_3451` from every model arm. The known fail-open-to-0.0 defect (patch 03) **fired live
twice** during the gate — pre-patch each would have been a silent 0.0 canonical denominator zeroing
every model arm on that problem. And the run surfaced a NEW defect the intake never saw: **patch 04,
`is_passed` counts done/empty-output/exit-0 as a pass over the evaluator's 0/100 verdict** — a
correctness-inflation channel proven live, mandatory before any model arm. Mutation evidence
recorded both directions (6 failed pre-patch → 8 passed post-patch). Auditor-verified: 616-row
recount, zero fail-open in raw records, docker leftovers checked (none — image tag restored
pristine). Evidence + four patches: research `0cf315c3`. Root: :597 flipped. Adoption (:596) still
gated on arXiv 2607.01211 (:601) — not this lane's call.

## A9 gfx90a training viability CLEARED, A10 IQ2 decode attributed (mainB, GPU lane)

**A9 — the 29-day block was a false negative, not a missing dependency or a missing measurement.**
A prior session reported "no PyTorch on the host" from a `find / -maxdepth 6`, which structurally
cannot reach a venv `site-packages` at depth 8+. torch `2.5.1+rocm6.2` with `gfx90a`, trl `1.9.2`
and peft `0.20.0` were installed the whole time in `/mnt/raid0/llm/tools/geak-v1-rocm62-py312`.
Verified independently on-thread (real bf16 4096² GEMM on device) before trusting it.
Result: **PASS** — real Qwen2.5-0.5B, LoRA on attn+MLP, loss `0.9174 → 0.4077` (55.6%) in 60 steps,
7.3 steps/s, GPU at 100% and 2,984 MB sampled *during*. Stage 1 (random-init) FAILED its own
pre-registered rule and is reported failed, not retro-scored. Stage 2's criterion 2 also failed as
written: the cause was my fp32 gradient-norm overflowing on a finite ~1e34 value, and the
instrument change after a failed pre-registration is declared in the artifact rather than quietly
applied. **The spike is a real trap for W3(a)** — `clip_grad_norm_` norms in fp32 and returns `inf`
there, propagating a NaN scale into every parameter. `OP-4` closed; all three W3 gates now met.
4-bit QLoRA specifically still blocked: `bitsandbytes 0.50.0` imports and exposes `Linear4bit`
(both false positives) but ships no gfx90a code object for ROCm 6.2. Artifact:
`artifacts/gpu-aux-baselines/a9_gfx90a_training_viability_20260812.md`, commits `0df2f2e7`, `3e04c570`.

**A10 — answered without OP-11, and it corrected me the same day.** OP-11 declined (Option B): its
capability is a seeded *synthetic-shape* producer and the real-model path needs none. Governed
rocprof-v1 run on the real 122B UD-IQ2_M passed (prefill, 732.8 t/s, IQ2_XXS `mul_mat_q` = 40.0% of
kernel time); non-governed decode run at `-n 128` gave 39.40 t/s with IQ2_XXS `mul_mat_vec_q` at
**16.42%** of decode kernel time, median 85.60 µs across 6,063 dispatches with a 6% spread.
**Correction:** an earlier claim today that IQ2_XXS MMVQ runs at maximum occupancy came from the
synthetic smoke's `<…,false,false>` variant at `Arch_VGPR=64`. Production MoE decode runs
`<…,true,false>` (mm_ids) at `Arch_VGPR=80` → **6 waves/SIMD, 75% of max**. A synthetic op capture
is a different kernel from the production one until the template arguments are compared. Scratch is
0 in both, so no spill. Artifact: `a10_iq2_decode_attribution_20260812.md`, commit `d7b404fd`.

**RVP-C2-11 filed with its fix attached** (`400caa91`): the rocprof-v1 runner hardcodes `-n 0`, so a
decode question returns `status: passed` carrying 2 warmup GEMV dispatches instead of 45,021 — a
silent failure that reads as success. Patch written and mutation-verified, parked rather than
applied because the runner sits on another lane's branch with no promoted identity on research
`main`. **A second follow-up I had proposed was dropped after checking its premise:** K28's NO-GO is
a long-prefill GDN question measured at p2048/p8192/p32768, so its instrument matched its phenomenon
and re-labelling it would have filed a false defect on a correctly-closed item.

threads through `profile_command()`; receipt gains `gen_tokens` + `phase`). Parked as an artifact rather
than applied — the runner lives on `codex/ak-d39-provider-contract` in another lane's tree and has no

instrument matched its phenomenon; re-labelling it would have filed a false defect on a correctly-closed
item.

**Accidental but real: the catalogue was un-editable by the whole fleet.** `agents_reference_guard.sh`

**C49 measured again from the receiving end: 38 of 39 messages carried `action_required` with mainB in
`needs_routing_to`; one was actually for me. 97% false-positive**, up from 86% earlier the same day.

### Post-wrap-up: both operator green-lights executed

**`bitsandbytes` built from source — 4-bit QLoRA now works on gfx90a.** mainA terminated, CPU free
(load 10.85/96 cores, no benchmark processes — verified, not taken on report). Built at tag `0.50.0`,
`-DCOMPUTE_BACKEND=hip -DBNB_ROCM_ARCH=gfx90a`, `-j24` to leave headroom. **Verified with a real NF4
run rather than an import**, which is the check that caught the wheel: 168 `Linear4bit` modules,
451.3 MB footprint, loss `1.1122 → 0.4062` (63.5%) over 60 steps, 3,102 MB peak VRAM. Standing hazard:
the PyPI wheel ships only a ROCm 6.4 code object, so a reinstall from PyPI silently reverts this.

**IQ2 VGPR lever resolved statically — zero GPU time, as predicted.** Read from the shipped
`libggml-hip.so.0.16.0`. Method note worth keeping: `.hip_fatbin` holds **135 concatenated offload
bundles**, one per translation unit — parsing only the first returns 8 KB of a 3 MB section and the
wrong kernel. True counts are **63** (`<…,false,false>`, 8 waves) and **78** (`<…,true,false>`,
6 waves); `rocprof`'s 64/80 is allocation-granularity rounding.

Three results, and two of them overturn my own hypotheses:
- **The mm_ids wrapper is not the cost** — Q8_0 runs the identical wrapper in **25** VGPRs. The 78 is
  IQ2_XXS's own dequant state.
- **"Codebook ⇒ expensive" is also wrong** — IQ4_NL is 38 and IQ1_S 42, while *block* quant Q3_K is the
  table's worst at 88. Pressure tracks per-block state-machine complexity, not the lookup table.
- **IQ4_XS sits at exactly 64 on the same wrapper**, so the 8-wave target is proven reachable and
  IQ2_XXS is 14 registers over — a bounded, known-achievable target rather than a hypothesis.

Constraint recorded: spill is 0 in every variant, so there is no slack; a reduction attempt must report
`vgpr_spill_count` beside occupancy or it is not evidence. And this licenses only a *try* — decode is
bandwidth/latency-bound (85.60 µs median, 6% spread), so +2 waves helps only if latency remains to hide.
Artifact: `artifacts/gpu-aux-baselines/a10_iq2_vgpr_lever_20260812.md`.

### Disassembly diff closes the IQ2 lever; GRPO blocked on a version skew

**Where the 14 registers go — sign unpacking, not the codebook.** `llvm-objdump` on both kernels at the
identical `<_,1,true,false>` wrapper: IQ2_XXS is **1002 instructions vs IQ4_XS's 565**, and the entire
437 excess is vector ALU (`v_*` 758 vs 334) while scalar (210/203) and LDS (16/16) are near-identical.
**IQ2_XXS issues FEWER global loads than IQ4_XS (3 vs 5)** — the third independent confirmation that the
codebook gather is not the cost. The excess is `unpack_ksigns`/`__vcmpne4`/`__vsub4` lowering to ~272
sub-word ops (`v_or_b32_sdwa` 96, `v_lshlrev_b16` 96, `v_sub_i16` 64, `v_bfe_i32` 46), where IQ4_XS does
its byte manipulation with **48 `v_perm_b32`** that IQ2_XXS never uses. Concrete direction filed:
re-express the sign expansion via byte permute, experimental branch only.

**Method warning worth keeping:** the first search of all 135 code objects used `llvm-nm`, which does not
exist in this ROCm install. Piped through `2>/dev/null | grep -q`, a missing binary is silence
indistinguishable from "no matches" — catalogue face 8, in my own work an hour after adjudicating it.

**GRPO blocked on a named dependency, not on hardware.** `trl 1.9.2`'s `GRPOTrainer` imports `FSDPModule`
from `torch.distributed.fsdp`, absent in `torch 2.5.1`. The earlier "TRL API reachable" finding stands and
was not wrong — `SFTTrainer`/`SFTConfig` import fine; `GRPOTrainer` pulls a different module. Needs an
operator call: bumping torch in a venv shared with the AutoKernel GEAK tooling has blast radius.

### GRPO unblocked by upgrading torch, in an isolated ROCm 6.3 venv

**Root cause was a three-way skew, not the two-way one first reported.** `trl >= 0.20` imports
`FSDPModule` (torch >= 2.6, and the rocm6.2 wheel index tops out at torch 2.5.1). `trl 0.19.1` — the
last release without that import — then breaks against `transformers 5.15.0`, whose
`_is_package_available` returns a **tuple**, making trl's `if is_vllm_available():` truthy and forcing
an unguarded `import vllm`. A trl pin therefore meant downgrading transformers as well, so one upgrade
beat two downgrades. Measured before deciding: `pip show trl` reports `Required-by:` **empty** — nothing
in the venv depends on it — so the "shared venv blast radius" I had asserted was smaller than claimed;
the decision rested on torch's dependents (`accelerate`, `bitsandbytes`, `peft`), not trl's.

**Isolated venv, Option C.** `/mnt/raid0/llm/tools/train-rocm63-py313` (Python 3.13, torch
`2.7.1+rocm6.3`, trl `1.9.2`, 23 GB). Chose 2.7.1 as the *lowest* version clearing the gate, to minimise
drift from the host's ROCm 6.2. **A ROCm 6.3 wheel on a 6.2 host works** — verified rather than assumed:
`hip 6.3.42131`, MI210 visible, real bf16 GEMM on device.

**Split is deliberate:** `geak-v1-rocm62-py312` keeps SFT + 4-bit QLoRA (both verified, internally
consistent at ROCm 6.2); the 6.3 venv runs GRPO only and **must never run bitsandbytes** — the
source-built `libbitsandbytes_rocm62.so` has zero torch linkage and pulls hipBLAS from system
`/opt/rocm` 6.2, while the 6.3 wheel bundles its own, so loading both puts two ROCm runtimes in one
address space. Recorded in the handoff as a don't-consolidate note.

**GRPO result: mechanical viability only.** 8 steps, 11.3 samples/s, 3,276 MB peak VRAM, residency
sampled during, finite `grad_norm` 3.3–6.1. Reward `-5.667 → -4.854` is **noise**, not learning:
`clipped_ratio: 1` and `mean_terminated_length: 0` mean every completion hit the token cap, so the
length reward was unoptimizable by construction. Stated as such rather than reported as improvement.

Two process notes. The venv was created by a `||` fallback because `/usr/bin/python3.12` does not exist,
so it is **Python 3.13** despite the original `py312` directory name — renamed, with all 14 absolute
shebangs rewritten and residuals verified at 0. The first rename attempt used a bash `while read` loop
in a **fish** shell: it failed silently and left 14 stale paths while appearing to succeed.
