## `mainC` final lane — a P1 I raised, retracted, and then re-scoped correctly

**The claim**: "the production GPU `llama-server` silently runs CPU ggml." Raised P1. **Withdrawn.**

**The falsifier came from the operator, not from me**: *"we've been measuring ~50 t/s throughput for
qwen3.6-27B which would be impossible on CPU."* That is an order-of-magnitude contradiction and it was
available the whole time.

**Root cause of the false finding**: I ran `ldd` in *my own shell*, which carries only the ambient path,
and reported the result as production behaviour. `scripts/server/stack_env.py` **prepends** the GPU tree
for llama-server roles, and its own comment states the design — the ambient value *is* the CPU tree, kept
deliberately as a fallback, and "prepending wins for libraries the tree actually ships." `build-hip/bin`
ships all four. Re-run with the launcher's composed path, all four including `libggml-hip.so.0` resolve
from `build-hip/bin`. The HIP backend loads.

This is the failure class I catalogued all session — **the check was sound and its target was not** —
committed by me, on the loudest claim I made, hours after writing the rule about it.

### Re-verifying the residual I claimed survived — it was half wrong too

I then asserted the speech kernels were "still exposed." Verified properly, both directions:

| Layer | Result |
|---|---|
| `whisper-server` / `tts-server` under the **launcher's** composed env | all 4 ggml libs from their own tree — **correct** |
| Same binaries in an **ambient** shell | 3 libs from llama's ggml 0.16.0 + own 0.18.0 — **mixed generations** |
| `ld_library_path_mode: replace` for speech | **implemented**, not just documented — `stack_manifest.py:318` defaults to `replace` when `backend:` is set |
| Paths for speech services | **backend-derived** (`backend_ld_library_path`), not the manifest's empty list |
| `verify_ggml_linkage` | **True** for both, enforced at launch — `orchestrator_stack.py:2479` |
| `verify_speech_kernels.sh` launch-recipe check | **OK** for both kernels |

**The production speech path is not exposed.** Three independent layers hold it.

I nearly repeated the error a *third* time inside this very verification: my first attempt called
`build_service_env()` on the raw manifest spec, which reports `paths=[]` and reads as a production
defect. That skips the backend-resolution step the launcher performs first. Measuring the wrong
environment is apparently not a mistake you make once.

### What is genuinely real, and why there is nothing to fix in code

The **ambient shell environment is poisoned** — `verify_speech_kernels.sh` fails closed on exactly this
and prints the remediation. Anything hand-run from an agent shell (a benchmark, a `whisper-cli`) silently
mis-resolves.

The code fix already exists and is correct: `136894e8` (2026-07-31) dropped the CPU-only llama dirs from
the global `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`; both `/etc/environment` and `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` now carry the
clean value.

**It is committed but not deployed.** Container PID 1 started **2026-07-29 13:41**; the fix landed
**2026-07-31 16:04**. The container is two days older than its own fix and has never been rebuilt, so
every shell inherits the pre-fix `containerEnv` regardless of what the committed files say. A container
rebuild is operator-only (host/container lifecycle), so it is carried below rather than actioned.

Adding a detector would be redundant — `verify_speech_kernels.sh` already catches it, fails closed, names
the fix, and `session_init.sh` surfaces it every session. **Nothing to build; one thing to deploy.**

### Catalogue

This adds no new face. It is **face 12 — verified at one timestamp, read at another** — in its most
expensive form: a fix verified as committed, read as deployed, on a process 14 days stale. My own
standing note (`feedback_verify_current_stack_before_claiming_role_replacement`) covers it exactly.

**Operator item**: container rebuild to deploy `136894e8`. Production serving is unaffected either way;
what it fixes is hand-run speech measurement from agent shells.

## `mainC` — 56-item triage queue cleared, and two peer findings adjudicated at HEAD

The standing queue was **56 items → 0**. Senders: mainA (19), mainD (16), coordinator (11), auditor (5),
mainB (5) — **every one terminated or closed**, so most of it was a dead fleet's own status. Cleared in
four grouped `corr_ids` rows rather than 56, one per disposition class, because the answer really was the
same within each class.

**32 informational** (task-complete / status / ack) → `superseded`: durable in commits already, no live
counterparty. **14 findings**, none solo-routed to me — two substantive, both worked. **6 assignments** →
four already executed this session; the compute one accepted. **4 defects** → one verified, three FYI.

### The one real bug in the pile — and the obvious fix for it is wrong

mainD routed, without fixing, that `config_applicator.py` calls `registry.get_role(primary)` on a
registry that has no such method. **Re-derived at HEAD and CONFIRMED at runtime** (`hasattr → False`
against the live registry): every execution of that branch raised `AttributeError`. The line anchor had
rotted 1634 → 1648; the finding underneath had not — the documented pattern exactly.

**The tempting repair is a trap.** `ModelRegistry` *does* expose `get_role_config()`, so
`get_role → get_role_config` reads as the natural one-word fix. But `build_server_command` consumes the
value by **nested attribute access** — `role_config.model.full_path`, `.acceleration`, `.name`
(`orchestrator_stack.py:1353-1355`) — and `get_role_config()` returns a plain **dict**. That swap trades
an `AttributeError` on `registry.get_role` for an `AttributeError` on `dict.model`, one frame deeper and
later. Fixed with `RegistryLoader`, which returns the `RoleConfig` the consumer was written against and
is what `orchestrator_stack` itself uses. Orchestrator `cc9b52f6`; 68 tests green.

**Two lessons from mutation-testing my own test:**

1. **My first mutation was not faithful.** I reverted only the assignment and left the import, which
   tripped a *different* guard and reported "imported from nowhere visible" — a resolution error that
   hides the real diagnosis. Reverting **both** lines, the actual historical defect, fails with
   `load_registry() returns ModelRegistry, which has no get_role`. A mutation that fails for the wrong
   reason is not a passing mutation test; it is a second vacuity.
2. **The failure message is part of the test.** The first version failed *correctly* and *uselessly*. A
   tripwire whose message misleads the next reader is a tripwire someone eventually deletes.

Test pins the **contract**, not the spelling: it AST-resolves the callee bound to `registry` *and* the
module it is imported from, then asserts the object supports the exact attribute chain the consumer
performs. 3 collected (not 0), 2 fail under the faithful mutation.

I also adopted mainB's finding directly: when restoring after each mutation I used **targeted edits, never
a whole-file copy** — that is the REVERT sub-shape of the shared-file hazard, which hit the fleet twice in
two days and was recovered by luck both times.

### A P1 verified into a non-defect — the gate was right

mainA's *"DECISION-GRADE IS UNOBTAINABLE TODAY BY BOTH ROUTES"* is **partially true and its framing is
refuted.** Every fact holds; the conclusion does not. The host-health gate returns exactly one warning
because **uptime is 14.1 days against the 7-day constitutional limit**
(`measurement/protocols/kernel-research.md:150`). That is the gate doing its job, and
`--allow-host-health-warning` is *supposed* to downgrade rather than rescue.

Decisively, **mainA's own runbook** says *"no flag combination banks this run decision-grade **before a
reboot**."* The bus claim dropped the load-bearing qualifier and turned a host-state fact into a phantom
code defect. No production logic was changed.

**A real defect was found underneath it**, though: the `skip_clean_check` term at the per-cell site was
**completely untested** — deleting it left all 65 tests green, in the *unsafe* direction (a run passing
`--skip-clean-check` on a clean host would have banked itself decision-grade). Guarded in research
`0aceeb71`, mutation-killed, 66 green. The companion run-level mutation *survives correctly* — the rollup
re-derives the tier from cell rows — and that was documented rather than papered over.

**Filed as OP-23**: reboot, or accept non-decision-grade numbers. This gates the OP-21 contention re-bench
and every other benchmark, and reboots are operator-only.

Both compute rows are **STILL-NEEDED** (verified against the tree, not just the checkbox): UFH-03 is
blocked on `peft`/`trl` not being installed; WG-LFM-1 needs the LFM2.5-2.6B GGUFs pulled — neither has any
artifact on disk. One correction to the dispatch: its *"take ONE quarter"* phrasing is stale, because

locks are a *different, still-live* primitive, and that is the correct claim for offline training compute.

## PIP-01 — S4b executed, and the ONNX rerankers were running 1.8–2.3× slow

**Screening first, and it changed the job.** The PIP-01 row's next action is S5 (request-path ColBERT
reranking), whose gate this handoff has already answered three times: the 2026-06-14 representative
direct-tool sentinel synthesized 55 pages with 0 irrelevant, i.e. `<10%` at `>=50` pages, which the
handoff's own decision-fork table maps to **NO-GO for S5**. Implementing S5 would have contradicted the
gate. The related B13 claim (`onnxruntime` undeclared, `src/retrieval/cross_encoder.py` silently off) was
**already satisfied** — orchestrator `fa3daeac` declares `onnxruntime>=1.20.0` + `tokenizers>=0.22.2` with
`tests/unit/test_retrieval_deps.py` guarding it, and the encoders load for real (`cross_encoder` scores
`+8.60` vs `−11.03` on a relevant/irrelevant pair). The one genuinely open CPU-lane item was **S4b**.

**S4b done.** Staged `lightonai/LateOn` (1.39 GB) to `/mnt/raid0/llm/models/lateon-onnx-int8` via the
sanctioned export helper; its ONNX signature is identical to the deployed GTE graph, so it is a literal
drop-in. Measured 10 snippets/call, 25 calls, 3 interleaved rounds, all slots in one session:
**LateOn 200.2 ms, GTE 202.7 ms, Reason-mxbai-32M 49.1 ms**. LateOn meets the ≤200 ms target and is
latency-neutral vs GTE — its +2.55pp BEIR is free.

**The real find.** A rerank call is `1 + N` *sequential single-row* forward passes over ≤64 padded tokens,
and ONNX Runtime was defaulting to one intra-op thread per visible core — 192 of them — so nearly the whole
pool was coordination overhead. Bounding it to 8 is **1.8–2.3× faster on every slot** and stops each call
spinning up a 192-thread pool on a shared box. Landed as an explicit `SessionOptions` in
`colbert_reranker.py`, env-overridable, with a mutation-tested guard; rerank output is **bit-identical**
across thread counts, so this is pure latency, not a quality trade.

**Two numbers this handoff was carrying are now corrected.** The long-quoted *"GTE baseline 180 ms"*
predates the canonical harness and is not comparable to anything — the control that proves the machine
did not drift is Reason-mxbai reproducing its own 2026-06-14 harness number on the same runs where GTE
reads 368 ms. And S4c's recorded *"104 ms, misses the ≤80 ms target"* was an unbounded-pool measurement:
bounded, that slot reads **49.1 ms** and passes its gate.

**Routed, not done:** `src/retrieval/colbert_encoder.py` and `src/retrieval/cross_encoder.py` build their
sessions the same unbounded way and are **live by default** in KB-RAG — same fix, but they belong to
`internal-kb-rag.md` (K9). Filed there as S4d rather than executed across a handoff boundary.

## `mainC` — ONNX reranker: row refuted, but a live production defect found underneath

PIP-01's own premise is **refuted**: S5 (request-path reranking) is gated on a >20% irrelevant-page rate
at n>=50, and the sentinel returned **55 pages / 0 irrelevant**, which the handoff's own decision fork maps
to **NO-GO**. Implementing it would have contradicted the handoff. B13 was likewise already satisfied —
verified *in git* (`fa3daeac` declares `onnxruntime>=1.20.0` in `pyproject.toml`), not merely on disk.

That is now **five of the last thirteen** dispatched rows whose premise had already been overtaken. The
screener proves well-formed, never still-needed.

### The real finding: ONNX Runtime was oversubscribing every retrieval call

ORT defaults to one intra-op thread per visible core — **192 here** — for forward passes small enough that
the pool is nearly all coordination overhead. The reranker was fixed at `intra_op=8` (orchestrator
`19111369`, 1.8–2.3x on every slot). The routed follow-on was that `src/retrieval/colbert_encoder.py` and
`cross_encoder.py` build sessions the same unbounded way and are **live by default in KB-RAG**.

**Verified rather than inherited — and the inherited number would have been wrong.** The report said both
carried "the same 1.8–2.3x penalty" and implied both should take the reranker's 8. Measured:

| Consumer | Call shape | Best | Unbounded penalty |
|---|---|---|---|
| reranker | 1+N sequential single-row | 8 | 1.8–2.3× |
| `colbert_encoder.encode()` | ONE single-row pass | 4 (8 within 3%) | 1.96× |
| `cross_encoder.score_pairs()` | N rows in **one** `run()` | **16** | 2.03× @b10, 1.45× @b50 |

**16 wins at both batch sizes with 32 worse at both**, so it is a real optimum and not an edge of the
swept range. Copying the sibling's 8 into the batched path would have cost **~40% at batch=50** — a
regression that looks like tidy-up and that nothing else in the suite would catch. The penalty narrows as
batch grows (more parallel work to amortise coordination over) but never disappears.

Landed as orchestrator `faee5e75`, with `test_the_two_defaults_are_not_silently_unified` guarding the
*reason* the constants differ, since "harmonising" them is the obvious future mistake. Output is
**bit-identical** bounded vs unbounded (max abs delta 0.0, rank order preserved) — pure latency.

### Two near-misses in my own method, both caught by my own rules

1. **I measured `0.0 ms` and nearly reported it.** `score_pairs()` was returning `None` because I called
   it without `ensure_loaded()`. A `0.0 ms` reading is the vacuity signature, not a fast path.
2. **I then nearly filed `is_available()` as a fail-open defect** — it returns True while `_session` is
   None. Its docstring says *"exist on disk. Does not load."* The contract is correct and my invocation
   was wrong. Ruling out the test method before calling it a bug is exactly the standing rule, and I was
   one step from breaking it for the second time today. I did check the production path afterwards:
   `rerank()` calls `ensure_loaded()` and falls through cleanly, so there is no silent-off defect.

### Index defect I introduced and removed in the same session

I filed the host-reboot escalation as **OP-23 — a duplicate of the existing OP-16**, which already owned
exactly that decision. A second row for one decision is precisely what the index contract calls a defect,
and I wrote it while holding the rule. Merged into OP-16 (widened from "the IQK campaign" to *every*
decision-grade benchmark, incl. the OP-21 contention re-bench) and OP-23 deleted. Gate: 0 problems.

### Routed onward, not silently dropped

`S4b` passed (LateOn is latency-neutral vs GTE at −1.2%, so its +2.55pp BEIR is free) and two numbers the
handoff had been carrying for weeks are corrected in-file: the "GTE baseline 180 ms" predates the
canonical harness and is not comparable to anything, and S4c's "104 ms, misses ≤80 ms" was an
unbounded-pool artifact — bounded, that slot is 49.1 ms and **passes**.

Still open and worth scheduling: **LateOn ONNX↔PyLate numerical parity was skipped** (`--no-parity`, needs
torch/transformers/pylate outside the orchestrator venv). The graph loads and emits the expected 128-dim
output, but it has not been compared against a PyLate reference the way Reason-mxbai was (2.72e-03 max
cosine divergence). Worth closing before any A/B result built on LateOn is trusted.

## `mainC` — the OP-21 dry run paid for itself twice

The operator's call — *"is there any benefit to doing it now if we have to repeat it later? … Maybe a dry
run?"* — was correct, and stronger than "avoid a wasted number." A real run now would also have taken CPU
regions that UFH-03 and WG-LFM-1 need. Verdict: **NOT-READY**, for a reason nobody had written down.

### The harness cannot express the geometry the task is about

`contention_matrix.py run` selects instances via `_select_live_pair_instances`, which loops both roles'
live instances and **returns the first DISJOINT combination**. It takes **no geometry argument**, and no
`--geometry` / `--overlap` / `--pair-ports` flag exists. The overlapping fallback fires only when no
disjoint pair is live at all.

Under the standard `--numa-mode both` lineup it selects `(8080, 8285)` — which is **exactly the shipped
1.89 disjoint row**. So the post-reboot run, on a scarce clean host, would have re-answered the wrong
question and produced a number we already have. That is what the dry run bought.

The escape hatch exists: `bench-nway` reads ports straight from a hand-authored manifest with **no
disjointness check**, so it can pin the true overlapping halves `8080 + 8185`, and it runs `samples=3`
with CV instead of `run`'s hardcoded `samples: 1`.

### And the path we would have used was silently sign-biased

`_http_bench` returns `(0.0, 0.0)` on timeout. `par_time = max(par_a_el, par_b_el)` **discards** that leg
while `total_tokens` still counts both. Measured against the old arithmetic, 5 s solo legs, floor 0.85:

| scenario | ratio | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| both parallel legs 10 s (real 2× contention) | 1.00 | allow |
| **leg B timed out, leg A 10 s** | **1.00** | allow — *identical to a healthy run* |
| leg B timed out, leg A 5 s | 2.00 | allow |

I first wrote this up as "inflates the ratio". **The measurement corrected me**: when the failed leg is
the one that would have *dominated* `max()`, its timeout is not inflationary, it is **invisible** — the
output is indistinguishable from a healthy sample. The sharp claim is that the error is
**one-directional**: no input of this shape can produce a false `block`; every one reads `allow`. So the
harness reports contention as most benign exactly when a server is collapsing under load.

And 2×48 threads on 48 physical cores — the overlapping geometry this task exists to measure — is
precisely where the 180 s timeout bites. The defect fires hardest on the measurement most likely to be
trusted.

Fixed at **both** sites (orchestrator `792ef3d8`), `_bench_nway` included, because that is the path the
re-bench will use and an N-way set has more legs to lose. `cmd_run` degrades the pair into `skipped`
rather than aborting the sweep — a skip is visible in the artifact, a laundered `allow` is visible
nowhere. This only extends a rule the module already held for its pre-flight check (*"HTTP failures are
not throughput evidence"*) to the bench legs themselves.

Mutation-tested per site, with a compliant-path guard and an explicit case proving a merely **slow** leg
(0.7 t/s over 180 s) still measures — a refusal that swallowed the contention signal it exists to protect
would be its own defect. 125 contention tests green.

### Filed, not fixed

Two more from the dry run, both recorded as tasks: `run --roles …` **truncates the matrix** (`pairs` is
emitter-owned, so unmeasured rows are not carried forward — 3 in, 1 out; against the default output that
destroys 14 of 15 rows), and the artifact **carries no host-health provenance** (`_host_metadata()`
collects uptime and kernel, `_emit_yaml` writes only `host:` and discards both — a run at today's 14.1 d
uptime would be indistinguishable from a clean-host one).

I deliberately did **not** touch the geometry selector. Its correct shape — explicit `--pair-ports` vs
refuse-overlapping vs reroute-to-`n_way` — is determined by the still-open marker-polarity decision, which
is the gate owner's call, and a unilateral patch could conflict with it.

## `mainC` — LateOn parity PASSES, and the check found something 25× bigger than itself

**Parity verdict: PASS.** Max per-token cosine divergence **8.24e-03** against the `PARITY_TOLERANCE
= 1e-2` the sibling Reason-mxbai export was judged at. On the sibling's exact pooled metric LateOn scores
3.23e-03 vs mxbai's 2.72e-03 — same order, same gate.

**The decisive move was decomposing FP32 from INT8**, and it is worth stealing for future parity work:

| comparison | max per-token cos div |
|---|---|
| **FP32 export vs reference** | **2.38e-07** |
| INT8 export vs reference | 8.24e-03 |

FP32 matching at float32 epsilon answers the actual question — *does the graph compute what the reference
computes* — and attributes 100% of the INT8 residual to quantization rather than a wrong graph. Without
that split, 8.24e-03 is just a number you either accept or don't.

Verified against genuine `pylate.models.ColBERT`, not a reimplementation. Negative control: the deployed
GTE graph on identical ids diverges by 1.19–1.25, ~1000× the observed figure — a graph agreeing to 1e-3
cannot be GTE.

### My venv survey was vacuous and cost the fight I picked

I concluded pylate was uninstallable and spent effort routing around it. **pylate 1.4.0 was already
installed** in `/mnt/raid0/llm/epyc-orchestrator/.venv-reranker/` — only its `bin/python` symlink is
missing. My survey loop guarded every candidate with `[ -x "$v/bin/python" ] || continue`, so the one venv
that had what I needed was **silently skipped by the check meant to find it**. That is the catalogue's own
shape: a probe whose guard excludes the positive case, and whose empty result is indistinguishable from a
true negative. And the workaround was trivial anyway — `pip download pylate --no-deps` completes in **3
seconds**, because `--no-deps` skips the resolution that was backtracking.

### PREFIX-1 — the finding that matters more than the parity question

`colbert_encoder.encode(text, max_tokens)` has **no query/document parameter at all**, so it cannot
express the distinction even in principle, and it feeds raw tokenized text to the graph. Both LateOn and
the deployed GTE declare `query_prefix "[Q] "` / `document_prefix "[D] "` with dedicated **trained** token
ids 50368/50369 that pylate inserts at index 1. Measured on the reference model, no ONNX involved:

| perturbation | max abs ΔMaxSim | top-1 agreement |
|---|---|---|
| INT8 quantization | 6.60e-03 | 100% |
| **missing `[Q]`/`[D]` prefix** | **1.63e-01** | **62.5%** |

**25× more perturbing than the quantization we just spent a subagent validating**, and it changes top-1 on
37.5% of queries. I went looking for a 1e-2 numerical question and found a 1.6e-1 semantic one.

**It is deliberately NOT fixed, and the reason is structural.** Indexing (`kb_rag.py:311`, `:434`) and
query (`kb_rag.py:567`) both use the same prefix-less path, so they are mutually **consistent** — just
off-distribution from training. Adding `[Q]` to queries alone would mismatch the stored index and make
retrieval **strictly worse**. Correct adoption means re-embedding the entire KB corpus. That is a
migration with a real cost and a behaviour change, not a patch — filed as **OP-24** with the measurement
attached.

**PREFIX-2, the consequence for the model swap**: the BEIR figures this handoff reasons about (GTE 54.67,
ColBERT-Zero 55.43, the LateOn delta) are **published vendor scores**, measured with the models used as
designed — prefixed. Production runs them unprefixed. A swap chosen on those deltas is chasing a gain
measured under conditions our deployment does not reproduce. It probably does not bias the *relative* A/B,
since the defect hits both arms equally, but the absolute gain is unproven here and should not be treated
as a production forecast until OP-24 resolves.

## `mainC` — WG-LFM-1 scout: the numbers favour LFM, and I nearly mis-adjudicated the reason they might not

GGUFs downloaded and **SHA-256-verified against the remote LFS oids** at the pinned revision. Raw decode
on one region (`q0`, 24 threads), canonical OMP + `GGML_IQK=1`, v9 `0db32c06e`:

| arm | pp512 | tg512 | peak RSS |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| LFM2.5-2.6B Q4_K_M | 1186.57 | **72.04** | 1.70 GiB |
| LFM2.5-2.6B Q8_0 | 898.89 | **44.95** | 2.81 GiB |
| gemma4-26B-A4B Q4_K_M (no MTP) | 555.28 | **28.00** | 16.51 GiB |

Crediting the incumbent a conservative 1.4–1.5× for MTP puts it at ~39–42, so LFM Q4 leads ~1.7–1.8× at
**9.7× smaller resident**, and LFM Q8 is roughly at parity. **The MTP credit is an estimate, not a
measurement**, and it is the load-bearing number in that comparison.

Row deliberately left OPEN — it asks for tool compliance, per-suite success and an MTP-inclusive
incumbent, none of which this window could produce. Scout-grade, correctly labelled.

### I refuted a finding, then had to refute my own refutation

The subagent's third finding was that *LFM2.5 reasons by default and that is pure cost for
`worker_general`*. I checked it against the transcripts and found **both** models emitting a thinking
block on all 5 prompts, with gemma's transcript the LARGER one (3864 vs 3018 bytes). I wrote that the
finding was mis-framed and the concern symmetric.

**That was wrong, and wrong in the way I have been documenting all day.** `worker_general` sets
`enable_thinking: false` in `orchestration/derived/stack_priors.yaml`. The bench ran the default embedded
template with thinking **ON** for every arm. So the symmetry I "found" is an artifact of a bench
configuration the incumbent does not use in production — I compared against a non-production
configuration of the very thing I was checking. The original finding is not just correct, it is sharper
than stated: in production the incumbent emits **no** reasoning tokens while LFM2.5 emits 60–120 even for
"capital of Japan", not reliably bounded.

The raw t/s table is unaffected — `llama-bench` decode is template-independent. What is affected is the
only comparison that decides the verdict: **time to a correct answer, not decode rate.** A 1.7×
tokens-per-second lead is worth nothing against several times the tokens per task, and `worker_general` is
precisely the short high-volume path where that bites. Filed as WG-LFM-2 with one narrow, answerable
question: **can LFM2.5's reasoning be disabled the way the incumbent's is?** Corroborating, its 128K
`lfm2` vocab already rules it out as a spec-dec drafter, so it cannot claw throughput back the way the
incumbent does.

### The methodological find is the most reusable thing here

**`numactl --membind=<node>` is wrong even when the CPU grant is a single region.** Binding memory to
node 0 to "match" a `q0` cpuset looks obviously correct and confines a bandwidth-bound decode to one
node's channels. Same cores, same binary, switching only to `--interleave=all`: **gemma +42%, LFM Q8
+80%, LFM Q4 +195%**, with stddev collapsing from as much as **51% of the mean to under 1.5%**.

The variance is the real tell: `--membind` was not slow-but-stable, it was **unstable enough to have been
reported as either result**. `--interleave=all` is correct at *every* CPU scope; a partial-machine grant
does not license a partial-machine membind. Filed as RECIPE-NUMA-1 because `canonical_recipe.py` has no
region-scoped path, and the OP-21 contention re-bench is region-scoped work that would walk straight into
it.

Also confirmed: **`GGML_IQK_Q8_0=1` is load-bearing** — without it the Q8_0 arm logged no `[iqk] ACTIVE`
line at all (pp +61%, tg +58% when enabled). A Q8_0 bench omitting it is not top-optimized.

Co-residency disclosed rather than hidden: another session held `q1` for ~68 s of the incumbent arm's
window. Disjoint region, gemma stddev stayed at 2.0%, and it landed on the *incumbent* arm — so if
anything the LFM lead is marginally overstated.

### WG-LFM-2 partially answered at zero compute — and it weakens the LFM case

The deciding question was *"can LFM2.5's reasoning be disabled the way the incumbent's is?"* The scout had
already archived the GGUF-embedded template, so this needed no compute at all — just reading it.

**No generation-time thinking switch exists.** The template's `preserve_thinking` looked like the answer
and is not: it governs whether **prior** assistant turns keep their `<think>` blocks in the rendered
history — `{%- if not keep_thinking and "</think>" in content -%}` strips them from past messages — and
has no bearing on whether the model reasons on the current turn. Every `/think` occurrence is a closing
`</think>` tag, not a `/no_think` control token. The mechanism production uses to silence the incumbent,
`chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking`, has **no counterpart**.

**The bound on that conclusion, stated rather than glossed**: absence from the template does not prove
the behaviour is unsuppressible — a model may still obey a system-prompt instruction without template
support. What it establishes is that the *production* mechanism does not transfer, so adoption would need
a different and currently unproven suppression path.

Net: the raw 1.7–1.8× decode lead is measured against an incumbent that emits **no** reasoning tokens in
production, while LFM2.5 has no demonstrated way to stop emitting them. That materially weakens the case,
and it is worth noting the whole chain — bench artifact → my false symmetry → production config → template
read — turned on one config key nobody had checked.

### Quantifying it: a 1.7× rate advantage does not survive a 27× token disadvantage

Derived from the committed correctness transcripts at zero additional compute (~4 chars/token):

| model | reasoning tok | answer tok | overhead |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| LFM2.5 Q4_K_M | ~384 | ~14 | **27.4×** |
| gemma4-26B-A4B, thinking ON (*bench only, not production*) | ~599 | ~12 | 49.9× |

The second row is the interesting one: **the incumbent's reasoning is MORE verbose than LFM2.5's.** That
is very plausibly *why* production sets `enable_thinking: false` for this role — and it is exactly the
lever LFM2.5 does not have. On these five prompts LFM spends ~9.4 s of decode to deliver ~14 tokens of
answer; a thinking-off incumbent at ~40 t/s delivers its ~12 in ~0.3 s.

**Three limits, stated so the number is not over-read.** These prompts are deliberately terse ("reply
with only the city name"), which is the *worst* case for a reasoning model — a fixed reasoning block
amortised over a 1-token answer — so real traffic will show a smaller ratio. The ~4 chars/token proxy is
approximate and the two models use different tokenizers, so cross-model counts are indicative only; the
robust figure is LFM's 27.4× against **its own** answer length. And the incumbent's thinking-off side is
inferred from config rather than measured. The direction is not in doubt; the magnitude is
prompt-dependent.

This is the whole reason the row stays open. A decode-rate table is not a verdict for a role whose work is
short and high-volume, and the scout was right to refuse the checkbox.

The invalid r4 campaign exposed two coupled implementation gaps: intermediate controller feedback ran
outside the parent worker's MI210 claim boundary, and same-UID controllers/candidates lacked an OS
isolation boundary. Continuing the campaign would therefore have spent inference on evidence that could
not select a controller. The repair is integrated on research branch
`integrate/inf03-isolation-merge-20260812` at `de7214ee` (not promoted by this root-only wrap-up).

| Surface | Durable outcome | Verification / evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Parent-owned evaluation | Every intermediate/final candidate request crosses an authenticated, exact-PID/start-tick AF_UNIX broker. The parent alone owns the serialized MI210 claim, sampler, evaluator subprocess, and durable request/result/baseline/lifecycle receipts | Research `d0d1896b`, merged at `70a0d254`; evaluator restore rehashes every evidence edge |
| Controller + candidate isolation | Each non-baseline controller runs in an exact-read/exec, device-blind, cgroup-owned sandbox with one inherited peer-bound broker socket. Candidate evaluation runs separately in a fresh GPU-capable deny-network sandbox | Research `9480a1b3`, `9a17e638`, `de7214ee`; focused warning-strict **95/95 PASS** |
| Exact EvoEngineer | The MIT paper-era source is pinned at `1649715a975b9022c84b5279c88aaef0b73b28dc`; the adapter preserves EvoEngineer-Full's four ordered fallbacks and exercises the exact upstream `EvoEngineer.run()` for 10 generations / 40 samples with fixture bridges only | Research `f42b3540`, `7e915831`, `a81c2692`; pinned Evo suite **11/11 PASS**; pinned controller suite **572/572 PASS** |
| Physical static readiness | Available-source audit schema v2 is **ready 7/7** over four representative tasks on physical gfx90a with zero refusal reasons and no controller/GPU command. The separate full audit refuses at 7/8 only for ARGUS | Available: `/mnt/raid0/llm/autokernel/probes/inf03-isolation-available-source-reaudit-20260812-r3/receipt.json`, self/file hashes `5fa93695…` / `082d977f…`; full: `...-r2/full-eight-arm-refusal.json`, self/file hashes `39a5bdd7…` / `c07c7787…` |
| Shared research clone | A stale mid-cherry-pick of `9c29064b` blocked every lane from committing. The AutoKernel owner aborted it after verifying the conflicted state; `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` and all unmerged paths are now absent, while mainA's staged A7 files were preserved and subsequently committed by that lane | Root progress correction `8ce46e31`; no parked lane patch was overwritten |
| Kernel-R&D dashboard | The readiness projector now prefers audit schema v2 from `receipt.json`, exposes the 7/7 panel, keeps v1 six-arm evidence only as a historical fallback, and retains the separate invalid-r4 disposition | `tests.test_dashboard_panels` and `tests.test_dashboard_static_js` in the root wrap validation |

No inference, model call, build, benchmark, or GPU command ran during this wrap-up or its static audit.
The system-Python discovery error (`scipy` absent) is not a live-arm defect: EvoEngineer is explicitly
pinned to `/mnt/raid0/llm/tools/geak-v1-rocm62-py312/bin/python`, where its tests pass. The all-tree
discovery under that pinned runtime is **not green**: a pre-existing import-time CLI parser rejects the
`--evaluate-existing`/`--execute` combination and exits before unittest emits a summary. That collection
defect is now an explicit INF-03 checkbox rather than being hidden by the focused green suites.

Next empirical gate: run a fresh one-task/one-arm engineering pilot and require non-null intermediate
feedback plus complete broker peer, controller/evaluator activation, MI210 claim/release, sampler, and
teardown evidence. Only then start the 7/7 availability-conditioned diagnostic panel. R4 remains
immutable invalid history and is never resumed, ranked, aggregated, or banked. ARGUS alone remains the
external exact-source dependency for the separate full 8/8 comparison.

## AutoKernel checkpoint — Claude Landlock repair probe and r5/r6 campaign differential

The fresh seven-arm campaign did not reach a live controller panel. R5 exposed a real Claude CLI
`-11`/SIGSEGV at the first actor-critic planner call after its baseline. A narrow Landlock trace found
ten required volatile runtime reads; research commit `916cdc92fcbb71e4b2fd937ffd4576f09e8c5d58`
(parent `15c61ed5`, nine files, +324/-33) admits only those fixed reads, keeps `/dev/urandom`
read-only, stages only the required Claude credential/config files, hashes their identities, and
scrubs the staged directory after execution.

## AutoKernel checkpoint — per-process model broker repair and INF-03 r7/r8

The r6 campaign/standalone differential was process identity, not another missing pathname. Landlock
resolved the controller's literal `/proc/self/*` grants against the sandboxed parent PID; a forked model
child therefore received `EACCES` for its own `/proc/self/stat`. Research commit
`43ba62632059938748766b2a7da231f3f8b2cca5` repairs the architecture: the controller is a deny-network
broker-only process, each read-only Claude/Codex client is sandboxed directly so volatile self paths bind
to the actual CLI PID, and the writable Codex actor retains its digest-pinned Docker boundary. The same
parent broker journals serialized model and evaluation receipts and centrally owns Arena evaluation.
Targeted validation passed **77/77**; the pinned full AutoKernel suite passed **5,655 tests** with one
expected failure. Research commit `a3fcea3c5352b88b7cd672db9a0210870bf3d395` then refreshed the
campaign's Claude/Codex actor source pin. Both research commits are clean local commits awaiting the
fleet's serialized publication path.

roughly +0.6% result as inside noise. This is live evidence that measured critique changes the next
proposal. It is not evidence of a kernel speedup.

### AK-WM pre-reboot audit: six implementation defects filed

The matched-archive path is not yet ready to spend the reboot-gated CPU campaign. A current-source audit
found six concrete defects, all now explicit open tasks under AK-WM-2a:

   native outcomes.

6. No exact repository-relative command regenerates both final proposal-v4 input trees byte-for-byte.

These are implementation tasks, not operator decisions. They must be repaired and the proposal-v4 inputs
regenerated before reboot so the next live intervention/control pair can materialize a usable real archive.

This section supersedes the terminal interpretation implied by the preceding r14 live snapshot and root

### R14 terminated invalid and cleaned up

R14's audit bound the shared source worktree while it was clean at `152ed0d9`, but that worktree advanced
to `03f9ae69` while the actor cell was running. Nine model calls returned zero without timeout and four
parent-owned evaluator windows compiled and passed correctness 4/4, with diagnostic averages
`1.0059882860636864`, `0.9942113624251266`, `1.0053314038021701`, and
`1.0015826675133939`. The outer guard then raised the exact refusal
`task or controller source identity changed during checkpoint execution`. Consequently no actor cell
receipt or aggregate was admitted; none of those intermediate ratios can authorize comparison, ranking,
belief, bank, champion, promotion, or release.

The terminal worker-result file SHA-256 is
`df0a4b5699c6267ee7b7649fb6d58e809bed4ac9c55160790457dcd194a78033`. The controller teardown file/self
hashes are `68c12e1962de96814c6b4f3dce50f50a80fc77a81547d210fa4a3fee8b6a4d1d` /
`261e52fb8393ba204172de8d9e17ed711ce43d951d8d69ac1a88ebd755a11576`. All eight distinct device claims
have released receipts. Every one of the 14 captured PIDs was absent on exact-PID recheck, and all 12
distinct recorded cgroups report both `verified_empty=true` and `removed=true`. R14 is immutable,
terminal-invalid, and non-rankable.

### The sixth pre-reboot implementation defect is closed and published

Research commit `03f9ae69afb8932da8f0d383cab9b78fc09660ca` adds a held-out projector that derives evidence only
from a distinct clean, hypothesis-bound completed journal and rejects synthetic, torn, or identity-drifted
inputs. It also adds the deterministic non-executing IQK pair generator: from one manifest it atomically

envelope trees; derives their common experiment frame; proves `ggml_iqk` is the sole changed factor;
records every input/output hash; refuses overwrite/drift; and rolls the pair back on failure. Its regression
test reconstructs a byte-identical pair from the same manifest. The broad AutoKernel suite passed **5,678
tests with one expected failure**.

The clean 18-commit AutoKernel series was conflict-guardedly promoted to research `main` as merge
`4328c37c940d125d7b11b4264b65f415c300eda0` (`Merge AutoKernel research implementation into main
(wrap-up promotion 2026-08-12)`). No second research promotion is required. The final real IQK pair is not
yet materialized because its authoritative held-out and intervention journals must come from the post-
reboot empirical campaigns; synthetic fixtures remain regression evidence only.

At this checkpoint the baseline receipt was admitted, the actor cell remained in progress, and its first

### AutoKernel ROCm phase repair and semantic-index correction

The governed rocprof-v1 attribution producer had silently hardcoded `llama-bench -n 0`, so a caller
could pose a decode question and receive a successful prefill-only receipt. Research commit
`c38bf49a` adds a validated `--gen-tokens` surface, threads it to the measured command, rejects
negative values, and records both the exact generation count and `prefill` versus `prefill+decode`
phase. The focused producer suite passed 12/12, the AutoKernel README suite passed 17/17, and the
repair was promoted to research `main` as `1527ea49` without running inference.

A subsequent root audit found that the AutoKernel checkpoint ancestry had resurrected resolved
operator rows OP-4 and OP-11 even though the structural index checker still passed. The broad merge
between published root and the 53-commit local-main side was conflict-guardedly aborted; all local
commits are backed up at `origin/reconcile/root-main-split-20260812`. This narrow correction removes
the two resolved rows, retains OP-16 as the real reboot decision, closes only the profiler
implementation, and files the still-required governed 122B decode replay as RVP-C4-4a. It also removes
the stale INF-22 dependency: the completed serving-runtime adapter is explicitly independent of the
current CPU IQK/archive path. It does not
claim that the wider split history has been reconciled.

The durable target order is now explicit: llama-owned kernels/dispatch; shape-aware
rocBLAS/hipBLASLt/CK selection; standalone Triton/HIP replacements integrated through experimental

champion source. The existing provider validator closes identity/prefix/champion admission only;

as AK-D39-2.
